# Finite-cutoff Gaussian coupling and conditional moments

## Annex N. Selected histories in the original covariance

### 1. Removing a rare selected mark at a fixed cut

Work at finite cutoff with the original source-free kernels and deterministic subtraction of V9.4. At each cut \(k\), use precisely the output \(X_k\) in that transport. For example, the output in H7.1–2 is the physical \(V\), and its carrier label \(\gamma\) is integrated with its posterior weight. In the path law obtained by composing the original kernels, \(F_k^i=\mathbb E[F_0^i\mid X_k]\) by induction and disintegration, for each root \(i=x,y\). History flags from an operation can be sampled as auxiliary observables from their joint disintegration with its input and output. They do not enlarge the conditioning sigma algebra. A selector taken from a separate history construction qualifies only where it has the stated probability bound in this path law, or where its joint lift has the same marked cut marginals. Write \(|F_0^i|\le B_i\) and keep the common translation fixed for now.

For a path event \(A_i\) with probability \(p_i\), define the two posterior numerators

\[
M_k^i=\mathbb E[{\bf1}_{A_i}F_0^i\mid X_k],\qquad
R_k^i=\mathbb E[{\bf1}_{A_i^c}F_0^i\mid X_k].
\]

Then \(F_k^i=M_k^i+R_k^i\), \(\|M_k^i\|_1\le B_i p_i\), and \(\|R_k^i\|_\infty,\|F_k^i\|_\infty\le B_i\). These statements include the posterior probabilities of both selectors. The events can differ at the two roots and can overlap.

Let \(D_k(U,V)=Q_k(UV)-(Q_kU)(Q_kV)\). For any integrable \(U\) and bounded \(V\), positivity, preservation of the integral and the \(L^\infty\) contraction give

\[
\mathbb E|D_k(U,V)|\le 2\|U\|_1\|V\|_\infty.
\]

Apply this once to \((M_k^x,F_k^y)\) and once to \((R_k^x,M_k^y)\). Bilinearity gives

\[
\mathbb E\big|D_k(F_k^x,F_k^y)-D_k(R_k^x,R_k^y)\big|
\le 2B_xB_y(p_x+p_y). \tag{N.1}
\]

The same proof gives the same bound for the difference of **terminal covariances**, using ordinary covariance in place of \(D_k\). Taking two copies of the same root gives the bound \(4B_x^2p_x\) for the difference of terminal variances. In particular the mixed terms with the unrestricted complement retain a full factor of the event probability. The argument uses the deterministic microscopic envelope on that complement.

Equation (N.1) applies separately at every cut, including a cut before \(A_i\) can be decided. The quantity compared there is \(D_k(R_k^x,R_k^y)\), computed with the original \(Q_k\). No consistency \(Q_kR_k^i=R_{k+1}^i\) is required or used. In particular no extra screening of past labels by a physical coarse output has to be inferred. Later births, failed preparations, mixed boundaries and resets are all integrated in the expectation.

### 2. A cutoff-dependent scale at which H9 pays for all the cross terms

Assume the H9.29 envelope with fixed \(s\ge0\), \(a=\ell_0 L^{-K}\), \(T(a)=1+\log(\ell_0/a)\), and \(B_i=A_i^{\rm env}a^{-4}T(a)^s\). Let \(A_i\) be the event that the root lies in the H9-F unresolved ancestry at a specified scale \(j\ge J\), using the root convention of H9.28–32. Until \(J\) this requires the unchanged determining stencils of H9. After \(J\) the selected ancestry must remain clean through \(j\). No condition is imposed after \(j\). H9.27–28 give, uniformly in the root and translate,

\[
p_i\le C e^{-\rho_j},\qquad
\rho_j=c\Gamma_*\,2^{\lfloor(j-J)/w_J\rfloor}.
\]

Fix \(u\ge0\), and put

\[
d_K(u)=8K\log L+2s\log(1+K\log L)+\log(K+1)+u.
\]

If \(\rho_j\ge d_K(u)\), the sum of the left side of (N.1) over \(0\le k<K\), **plus** the absolute change in terminal covariance, is at most

\[
4C A_x^{\rm env}A_y^{\rm env}\ell_0^{-8}e^{-u}. \tag{N.2}
\]

Indeed, \( (K+1)a^{-8}T(a)^{2s}e^{-\rho_j}\le\ell_0^{-8}e^{-u}\). The same computation at one root controls the change in the terminal variance. The bounds survive the common translation average of V9.5. For \(0<r\le\ell_0\), \(\ell_0^{-8}\le r^{-8}\), so (N.2) fits the two-point budget without a further estimate of the distance between the roots.

For \(d_K(u)>0\) an explicit sufficient choice is

\[
j=J+w_J\max\{0,\lceil\log_2(d_K(u)/(c\Gamma_*))\rceil\},
\qquad j\le K. \tag{N.3}
\]

For fixed \(u\), under the standing trajectory with \(\beta=g_0^{-2}=O(1+K)\), \(R_0=O((1+\log\beta)^r)\), \(w_J=O(1+R_0)\), and the H9 weak threshold, this scale is \(O((1+\log(1+K))^{r+1})=o(K)\). Thus a class of roots with a long-lived clean initial or controlled-prefix ancestry can be carried through the entire remaining evolution, including arbitrary later contamination. The cutoff condition in (N.3) matters at finite \(K\). Histories which leave this class earlier, roots with uncontrolled entrance, and the terminal variance of their complementary old field still require estimates.

### 3. Summing the covariance with an unrestricted mark after a chosen cut

At a single cut \(j\), take two real marks \(U_j,V_j\) on the precise space of the original transport. Suppose
\(\|U_j\|_\infty\le B,\ \|U_j\|_1\le Bp,\ \|V_j\|_\infty\le D\), where \(0<p\le1\). For example, H9.29 and a history event of probability \(p\) give these bounds for its old posterior numerator at this cut. Propagate **these functions** by the original \(Q_k\) from \(j\) to \(K\). Then, with a numerical constant,

\[
\sum_{k=j}^{K-1}\mathbb E|D_k(U_k,V_k)|
+|\operatorname{Cov}(U_K,V_K)|
\le C B D\,p(1+\log(1/p)). \tag{N.4}
\]

This bound is independent of the length of the remaining trajectory. It applies to a decomposition initialized at \(j\), including \(F_j=U_j+(F_j-U_j)\). It requires no identification of a hidden past selector at any later cut.

**Proof.** The case of a zero envelope is immediate. Realize the composition of the original kernels from \(j\) to \(K\) on its canonical path space. In the order \(X_K,\ldots,X_j\), let \(\mathcal H_n=\sigma(X_K,\ldots,X_{K-n})\) and \(d=K-j\). Then \(v_n=V_{K-n}(X_{K-n})\) is a martingale, since the conditional law of the next finer state is the original backward kernel. The same holds for \(u_n=U_{K-n}(X_{K-n})\). Define the predictable sign \(\sigma_n\) of \(\mathbb E[\Delta u_n\Delta v_n\mid\mathcal H_{n-1}]\), with value zero at a tie. The conditional expectation inside this sign is \(D_{K-n}(U_{K-n},V_{K-n})(X_{K-n+1})\). For the transformed martingale \(S_m=\sum_{n=1}^m\sigma_n\Delta v_n\), orthogonality of increments gives

\[
\sum_{k=j}^{K-1}\mathbb E|D_k(U_k,V_k)|=\mathbb E[u_d S_d].
\]

For every stopping time \(\tau\le d\), conditional orthogonality gives
\(\mathbb E[(S_d-S_\tau)^2\mid\mathcal H_\tau]\le
\mathbb E[(v_d-v_\tau)^2\mid\mathcal H_\tau]\le 4D^2\).
The conditional maximal inequality for the square of the stopped martingale gives probability at most \(1/4\) of a subsequent displacement \(4D\) or greater from \(S_\tau\). Restart at the first such displacement. Each crossing overshoots by at most \(2D\), the maximum size of an increment. The probability of \(m\) crossings is at most \(4^{-m}\) by iteration. With fewer than \(m\) crossings, \(|S_d|<6Dm\). Thus \(\mathbb P(|S_d|>t)\le C e^{-ct/D}\), uniformly in \(d\).

For every \(t\ge0\),
\(\mathbb E[|u_d|{\bf1}_{\{|S_d|>t\}}]\le B\min(p,Ce^{-ct/D})\).
Integrating from zero to infinity bounds \(\mathbb E|u_d S_d|\) by \(CBDp(1+\log(1/p))\). Finally \(|\operatorname{Cov}(U_K,V_K)|\le2BDp\) by the original kernel contractions. This proves (N.4), with the zero-probability case obtained directly.

The logarithm is necessary using only these hypotheses. On a depth-\(d\) fair-coin tree in the martingale order above, take \(u_d\) to be the indicator of all heads, of probability \(p=2^{-d}\). Start \(v_0=1/3\); at a still-live node negate \(v\) on heads, triple it on the first tail, and freeze it after that tail. This is a martingale bounded by one. At step \(n\) the live node has probability \(2^{1-n}\), \(u_{n-1}=2^{n-1}p\) and \(|v_{n-1}|=1/3\). The conditional bracket has magnitude \(2u_{n-1}/3\) there and vanishes off that node. Thus the absolute sum is \(2dp/3\) on this finite normalized chain.

For a pair at distance \(a\le r\le\ell_0\), take \(BD=A_x^{\rm env}A_y^{\rm env}a^{-8}T(a)^{2s}\). If \(p\le e^{-\rho}\), monotonicity of \(p(1+\log(1/p))\) gives the sufficient condition

\[
\rho-\log(1+\rho)\ge
8\log(r/a)+2s\log(T(a)/T(r))+u .
\tag{N.5}
\]

Under (N.5) the entire remaining cross contribution in (N.4) is at most \(C A_x^{\rm env}A_y^{\rm env}r^{-8}T(r)^{2s}e^{-u}\). At \(r=\ell_0\) this also pays a mixed terminal covariance. At shorter distances it gives a less expensive condition for the defect sum. Applying (N.4) twice to the bilinear split in §1 gives the corresponding remainder estimate from \(j\) onward. One may absorb the fixed prefactor in H9.28 into \(\rho\). This criterion leaves both the complement-complement term and the defects before \(j\) to be estimated.

### 4. Later births and fixed boundaries

**An arbitrary base scale for a selected Q/R ancestry.** Fix the incoming step \(b\) and let \(t=b+1\). Use RB9.21 on its actual regular carrier and strictly admitted output. Select active cells whose components at \(t\) originate entirely in the current Q/R births. From \(t\) through the observation \(j\), allow clean mergers of these components. With \(w_b=O(1+R_t)\) the clean window of H8, a specified family \(S\) of current cells obeys

\[
\Pi(S\text{ belongs to the selected ancestry at }j)
\le \exp[-c\,p_0(g_b)^2\,2^{\lfloor(j-t)/w_b\rfloor}|S|].
\tag{N.6}
\]

At \(t\), the fixed layers and bounded rectangular fills in the construction of the active set put a Q/R test cube within a fixed number of cells of each selected cell. A fixed fraction have disjoint determining neighborhoods. The number of choices per cell is bounded, and RB9.21 pays for all chosen cubes **at that one step** before any later label is conditioned on. The clean two-witness geometry H8-A then iterates from \(t\), as in H9.27. Its embedding entropy is absorbed since \(p_0(g_b)^2\gg w_b\). A lower bound \(C_s(1+j)\) on the exponent per cell pays the age of two microscopic marks through \(j\); the ordinary component size and separation can still be charged from the unused reserve. This yields the selected scale sum (4) in the derivation below. For an unqualified later physical birth the choice of the family at \(b\) can depend on a later field, and this proof does not apply.

There is a chronological extension. After the one strictly regular physical birth layer, allow further Q/R births wherever the actual input passes the pre-gauge canonical-small test H9.37. Each such event forces a large fresh gauge variable. Conditional on the full pre-draw state, its normalized cost multiplies the earlier observed flags. Iterating from the last such draw leaves RB9.21 to apply once at the initial strict step. A bounded restart window can include failed preparations which retain the already traced active geometry. The window length and the entropy for its possible origins must be paid by the minimum per-cell exponent in the window. Derivation, source equations and window condition

**A current plaquette witness for P, and the boundary obstruction.** In the local P test the determining field is copied from the current coarse links. Nested block averaging of that copied field preserves the copy. Each extended plaquette in the mixed determining data is therefore trivial or a copy of a coarse plaquette. The variational theorem of CMP102, Theorem 1, together with the interior margin of the P test gives a depth-independent \(c_P>0\): a P-bad output forces some actual coarse plaquette of size at least \(c_P\varepsilon_j\) in the enlarged support. For a prior successful reset with uniformly small zero-fluctuation template on the complete determining cone, a bounded number of subsequent unchanged block averages then forces a large actual reset draw if this P test or the strict extension test fails. H8-D bounds that draw in its own normalized law.

Freeing every link of the offending plaquette can still leave the boundary in control of its curvature. In an SU(2) Wilson box of side \(d\) fix the tangential boundary links to constant Cartan flux \(\theta\), with \(d^2\theta<\pi\) and \(\theta>0\) small. In each 12-slice the boundary holonomy has angle \(d^2\theta\); the sum of elementary plaquette angles in any extension is at least this large by the bi-invariant triangle inequality. The Wilson cost \(1-\cos a\) has a uniform quadratic lower support about \(\theta\) for \(0\le a\le\pi\). Summing the support inequalities proves that every minimizer has angle \(\theta\) on every 12-plaquette. At fixed \(d\), with \(\theta=2p_0(\beta^{-1/2})/\sqrt{\beta}\), the exact conditional probability of angle at least \(\theta/2\) on a free interior plaquette tends to one. The boundary example concerns the necessary plaquette witness. The converse from a large plaquette to the P or preparation-failure label is unavailable. Proof, boundary conditions and flat-collar comparison

### 5. The early restart on the literal microscopic initialization

Write \(\beta=g_0^{-2}\) in the H9 source convention. The preparation described on pp.177–179 of LFR I uses \(N\) preceding steps, \(h=k-N\ge0\), and \(N>N_0\), where \(L^{N_0-1}=R_{k-N_0+1}\). At an attempted step \(k\le J\) this radius has an index in the prefix, where the trajectory and rounding give \(R_i\ge c(1+\log\beta)^r\). Consequently \(k\ge N>N_0\ge (r/\log L)\log\log\beta-O(1)\). Choose the freely shrinkable H9 constant \(0<\kappa<r/\log L\), also satisfying H9.20, and \(J=\lfloor\kappa\log\log\beta\rfloor\). At sufficiently weak coupling these inequalities exclude every such attempt up to \(J\). This uses the prescribed initialization at scale zero and the preceding-step condition in the actual operation. An initialization supplied with a pre-existing history requires its own check. Source-page argument

The unchanged-stencil condition in H9-C is therefore automatic for all ordinary births through this \(J\). Prior to an attempted replacement the current field passes through the preparatory identities at fixed current coordinate, as in H9 §1. H9-E's trace back to a fine plaquette or an actual gauge innovation now covers the full active set at \(J\) on this initialization. Thus it supplies a genuine initial set of whole active components with joint cell probability at most \(\exp(-q_0|S|)\), \(q_0=c\Gamma_*\), without selecting a clean subfamily during this prefix. This is a statement about occupancy. It does not declare the marked law on those components to be regular.

### 6. Repeated clean intervals interspersed with arbitrary processing

Fix a deterministic starting cut \(t\) and a random union of whole active components \(A\) there. Assume the actual normalized law satisfies \(\Pi(S\subset A)\le e^{-q|S|}\) for every fixed family of distinct cells. Measure physical distance using \(s_j=Mh_jR_j\). Fix \(H>0\). Use a common integer \(w=O(1+R_t)\) at least as large as every clean certification window after \(t\), which exists by H8.3 and the clean eligibility bound in H8. Take \(q\) larger than a sufficiently large constant times \(1+w+(\sup_{j\ge t}R_j)^4\). All cuts are restricted to the given finite weak trajectory.

Two operations on these selected sets have uniform bounds.

1. After \(m\) clean steps retain unresolved descendants whose entire unresolved ancestry since the given cut satisfies H8's clean conditions and comes from the chosen components. Mergers inside the selected class are allowed. H8-A implies an occupancy exponent at least \(c_1 q 2^{\lfloor m/w\rfloor}\). The binary witness uses only the joint probability for specified cells at the given cut. Distinct leaves across the fixed fraction of separated terminal roots and their embedding cost \(\exp(C w\,\#\mathrm{leaves})\) are exactly the ones in H8.8 and H9.27. Thus \(c_1>0\) is independent of the number of steps and of the distribution before that cut.
2. From a cut with selected set \(A'\) and exponent \(q'\), follow the original law for \(d\) steps with all its processing. At the observation cut \(l\) keep **whole actual active components for which every cell center is within \(H s_l\) of some cell center of \(A'\)**. For each specified observation cell there are at most \(\exp(C_H(1+d))\) candidate finer cells. A fixed fraction of the observation cells have disjoint candidate neighborhoods, by a geometric coloring and H8.3. Summing assignments of distinct witnesses at the previous cut gives exponent at least \(c_H[q'-C_H(1+d)]\). This includes arbitrary mixed births, failed preparations, replacements and mergers within this spatial selection. A newly created component can qualify by proximity alone. A long component with only one cell near \(A'\) does not qualify.

Choose a fixed integer \(n\) so large that \(c_Hc_1 2^n\ge4\), increasing the initial weak threshold so that \(c_HC_H(1+w)\le q\). At each cycle take \(nw\) clean steps by rule 1 and then \(w\) steps by rule 2. Let \(t_i=t+i(n+1)w\) be the complete cycle endpoints, and \(A_i\) their recursively selected unions. For every \(i\) with \(t_i\le K\),

\[
\Pi(S\subset A_i)\le \exp[-2^i q|S|]. \tag{N.7}
\]

In fact, assuming exponent \(q_i=2^iq\), the intermediate clean endpoint has exponent \(q'\ge c_1 2^n q_i\). Rule 2 gives \(c_H[q'-C_H(1+w)]\ge 2q_i\) for the next full components. Induction uses unconditional cell bounds at the previous cut. It never multiplies conditional probabilities of physical birth layers. The schedule and its constants are fixed before the realization. Partial cycles use the corresponding clean or proximity rule at the observation cut; endpoint membership by itself supplies no estimate on an intermediate mark.

For the microscopic restart \(t=J,\ q=c\Gamma_*\) of §5, the hypotheses follow from H9.22 and \(J+w=o(\Gamma_*)\). Throughout cycle \(i\) the exponent per cell is at least \(c'2^iq\), which, at a sufficiently weak fixed threshold, pays the bare two-mark age \(D_s j\) of H8.14 as well as the ordinary size of a connected component. Indeed \(j\le J+(i+1)(n+1)w\) and \(i+1\le 2^i\). The connected-animal sum for a component containing both supports retains a factor \(Ce^{-c''q}\exp[-c r/h_j]\), since \(2^iq\gg R_j^4\) and a connecting component requires at least \(c(1+r/s_j)\) cells. The remaining engineering factor is \(A_x^{\rm env}A_y^{\rm env}h_j^{-8}T(h_j)^{2s}\). The geometric sum over \(h_j\ge r\) is dominated by its finest scale, and for \(h_j<r\) the exponential pays the eighth power and logarithm. This gives the selected same-component bound \(C_s A_x^{\rm env}A_y^{\rm env}e^{-c''q}r^{-8}T(r)^{2s}\) over the trajectory, for \(a\le r\le\ell_0\) (with constants decreased in the exponents as necessary).

There is also a cross-covariance consequence on the original kernels. Apply §§1–2 to the event that a specified root cell belongs to \(A_i\), using a fixed finite collar if required. At an endpoint with \(2^iq\ge d_K(u)\) the absolute defect and terminal covariance costs in (N.2) are paid, up to the fixed collar multiplicity. For fixed \(u\) it suffices to reach \(J+O(w\log(K+1))\), if that endpoint lies in the trajectory. Subsequent processing is unrestricted. This handles arbitrarily many prescribed interruptions of clean evolution as \(K\) increases, together with the covariance against the unselected mark once the rarity reaches the stated threshold. Mixed histories whose components leave the proximity selection, and the remainder-remainder terms, are open.

### 7. Conditional boundary tests on the actual strict RB9 parent

Fix \(H,W\) on the regular, strictly admitted fiber RB9.6 before the Q/R tests. Write \(p=p_0(g_k)\) and \(R=R_k\). Select disjoint deterministic sets \(D_i\) of independent starred-bond coordinates, each of size at most \(C_0 R^4\), separated by at least \(A R\) in current lattice units. Choose fixed \(A\) larger than the inherited support-diameter constant in RB9.7. The witness set for a chosen test cell must be contained in its \(D_i\). All coordinates outside \(\bigcup_i D_i\) are now revealed; call their actual values \(e\). The estimates below integrate \(e\) in the very same fiber. On a particular \(e\) a cutoff may pin the conditional law arbitrarily close to its boundary.

**Comparison between separated boxes.** Denote the conditional law by \(\mu_e\). On almost every \(e\) of its true marginal, for nonnegative functions \(f_i\) of the \(i\)-th box and this exterior,

\[
e^{-2\sum_{i\in I}\delta_i}\prod_{i\in I}\mu_e(f_i)
\ \le\ \mu_e\!\left(\prod_{i\in I} f_i\right)
\ \le\ e^{2\sum_{i\in I}\delta_i}\prod_{i\in I}\mu_e(f_i)
\quad\text{for every subfamily }I, \qquad
\max_i\delta_i=o(p^2). \tag{N.8}
\]

To see this, fix the variables in all the other boxes as well. Each hard factor touching \(D_i\) depends only on that box and \(e\). Thus the permitted support in \(D_i\) is common for all such settings. Keep in a single-box reference density all these cutoffs, the outer box, the quadratic coupling to \(e\), and the internal quadratic form. RB9.8 bounds the sum of norms of real localized potential terms touching \(D_i\) by \(C|D_i|v_k\) on \(D_H\), where \(v_k\to0\). Dropping a term independent of the variable under consideration has no effect. All quadratic terms coupling \(D_i\) to another box have oscillation at most \(C|D_i|p^2 e^{-\gamma A R}\) by the exponentially summable precision rows and the actual envelope \(|A_b|\le C_Hp\). The sum here is over the entire distant region, independently of the number of boxes. If the localization estimate is given for the covariance \(C=M^{-1}\), exponential row and column sums and the uniform bound on \(\|M\|\) in RB9 §§4–5 imply the precision estimate at a smaller rate. Indeed conjugating \(C\) by \(e^{\alpha d(\cdot,y)}\) changes its operator norm by \(O(\alpha)\) in the sense \(\|e^{\alpha d}Ce^{-\alpha d}-C\|\le C'\alpha\). Inverting at small fixed \(\alpha\) gives \(|M_{xy}|\le C''e^{-\alpha d(x,y)}\); the fixed-dimensional lattice row sum follows at a smaller exponent.

Take \(\delta_i=C|D_i|(v_k+p^2 e^{-\gamma A R})\). Since \(R^4=o(p^2)\), this is \(o(p^2)\) uniformly at weak coupling. The conditional single-box law lies between \(e^{-\delta_i}\) and \(e^{\delta_i}\) times the normalized reference. This likelihood comparison uses oscillation on the true support and needs no volume lower bound on its allowed region. Integrating the same inequalities with the other boxes unrevealed also bounds \(\mu_e(f_i)\). Therefore the conditional expectation given any subset of the other boxes differs from \(\mu_e(f_i)\) by factors at most \(e^{\pm2\delta_i}\). Successive conditioning proves (N.8).

**Exceptional exteriors and actual witness probabilities.** Let \(0<u\le u_*\) from RB9.15, \(Z_i=\exp(u\sum_{b\in D_i}|A_b|^2)\), and \(m_i(e)=\mu_e(Z_i)\). For every specified family \(I\), (N.8) and RB9.15 give

\[
\mathbb E_e\prod_{i\in I}m_i(e)
\le\exp\!\left(2\sum_{i\in I}\delta_i+C\sum_{i\in I}|D_i|\right).
\tag{N.9}
\]

On this carrier RB9.19 forces an actual Q/R birth in its eligible cell to have a coordinate of size at least \(\alpha_0p\) in the indicated witness set for some fixed \(\alpha_0>0\). Use the local event \(B_i=\{\max_{b\in D_i}|A_b|\ge\alpha_0p\}\) as a majorant; the selector itself can depend on additional eligibility data. Choose \(0<\lambda<u\alpha_0^2/2\) and call box \(i\) exceptional on \(e\) if \(m_i(e)>\exp(\lambda p^2)\). Markov's inequality in (N.9) shows that every fixed family \(T\) is simultaneously exceptional with probability at most \(\exp(-\lambda p^2|T|/2)\) at a sufficiently weak threshold. If \(i\) is ordinary on \(e\), then \(\mu_e(B_i)\le\exp[-(u\alpha_0^2-\lambda)p^2]\). Applying the upper side of (N.8) to the ordinary boxes gives, for disjoint specified \(S,T\),

\[
\Pi_{H,W}\bigl(T\text{ exceptional},\ S\text{ ordinary},\
                 B_i\ \forall i\in S\bigr)
\le e^{-c p^2(|S|+|T|)}. \tag{N.10}
\]

The same estimate holds with the actual indicated Q/R births on the left. This is an annealed boundary estimate for revealing an arbitrarily large exterior inside the genuine strict pre-classification law. It simultaneously charges boxes whose conditional moments deteriorate and boxes whose physical fluctuations are large on an admitted exterior. Each charge can pay \(O(R^4)\) ordinary-size expense. Conditionings which introduce a historical potential or a cutoff belonging to another parent law still require separate admission. The estimate also gives no martingale product across two different physical birth depths.

### 8. What a genuinely mixed next observation retains

Suppose the actual unresolved geometry is empty at the incoming cut \(b\) on a strict RB9 parent. Let \(W\) be the physical \(b+1\) output before processing. Any new active component must contain a post-\(b\) creation in its unresolved ancestry. Apply the LFR I eligibility clock from §5 to the first alleged processing cut \(l\). By H8.3,
\(N(l)>N_0=1+\log_L R_{l-N_0+1}\ge1+\log_L(R_l/C_R)\).
Under the trajectory \(R_l\asymp R_b\) for \(l-b=O(\log(2+R_b))\): \(x_l=g_l^{-2}\) changes by \(O(l-b)\), and \(R_l\) is the rounded fixed power of \(1+\log x_l\). Consequently, for \(l-b\le a_b=\max(0,\lfloor\log_L R_b-C_*\rfloor)\), a post-\(b\) creation lies within the required clean history of every candidate. No such processing is possible. The physical fields through that window are the iterated averages of \(W\) up to gauge, including on branches with ordinary large-field flags.

If \(B\) denotes \(n\) specified initial strict Q/R births and \(f\ge0\) is bounded and gauge invariant separately in those later physical fields, the deterministic equality and RB9.21 give

\[
\mathbb E[{\bf1}_B f\mid H,W]\le e^{-c p_b^2 n}\mathbb E[f\mid H,W],
\qquad p_b=p_0(g_b). \tag{N.11}
\]

Actual later labels require an additional geometric argument. For example, at the next ordinary step a prescribed P test is a function of \(MW\) on its eligible region. For a Q test with strictly good surrounding P tests on the copied data of \(MW\), the comparison on an eligible cube uses \(W\) and the fresh soft-gauge variables. Earlier flags can remove its eligibility. The absence of all earlier flags in the finite enlarged collar is sufficient to restore eligibility, regardless of flags outside. For a prescribed family \(C\) of these later tests, let \(m\) count the earlier candidate cubes in their collars. On the entry described above, the true conditional probabilities satisfy

\[
\mathbb P(B\cap C\mid H,W)
\le \exp[-c p_b^2 n+C m R_b^4 e^{-c' p_b^2}]
\,\mathbb P(C\mid H,W). \tag{N.12}
\]

For the upper bound, use the hypothetical later test on \(W\) with its own fresh gauge variables whenever eligibility holds, and discard the old mask. For the lower comparison to \(\mathbb P(C\mid H,W)\), keep all candidate old flags absent. A sufficient event is that every coordinate of their enlarged RB9.19 witness sets lies below \(\alpha p_b\), for a fixed small \(\alpha>0\). Earlier vetoes of such individual coordinates add singleton cutoff factors satisfying RB9.7 with one additional overlap and inner radius \(\min(c_h,\alpha)p_b\). The proof of RB9.15 applies with those vetoes retained in both numerator and denominator, uniformly in their locations and number: each added defect forces its own coordinate beyond the inner radius and has support one. Adding the next veto thus costs probability at most \(Ce^{-c' p_b^2}\) in the preceding normalized law. Multiplication of the successive acceptance probabilities costs at most \(C m R_b^4e^{-c'p_b^2}\) in its negative logarithm. The new gauge tape has its original normalized product law. Together these comparisons prove (N.12). It supplies a likelihood comparison for the later event; the later event's own probability still has to be bounded. Eligibility, exact source tests and the collar proof

### 9. Old marks in two distinct live components

Consider two distinct live components \(Y_1,Y_2\) of the actual LFR II (1.103) integral. Fix the complete exterior \(e\), including the choice of these components and the live variables of all other cores if present. Use the real product representation for the old integration variables \(\xi_i\), with all terms depending on at most one of \(\xi_1,\xi_2\) absorbed into its corresponding probability measure. This disintegration and the relative-tree estimate are already established in the repo in T1 §7.80 and Lemma 7.194. The one-body probability measures can have arbitrary boundary-dependent means. On this fixed record the remaining density relative to their product is proportional to \(\exp\Psi_{12}\), and the linked terms have the bound

\[
\|\Psi_{12}\|_\infty\le
C\min(|\partial_M^+Y_1|,|\partial_M^+Y_2|)
\,e^{-\gamma d_M(Y_1,Y_2)}=:\delta .
\]

Here the boundaries and distances are in current native \(M\)-cells. An interaction incident on both integrations contains both bodies, and the relative tree meets exterior cells adjacent to each. Reserving a fraction of its length pays \(d_M(Y_1,Y_2)-O(1)\). The sum of the remaining supports can be rooted at either boundary, as in the proof of the fixed-family bound. This uses the source's localization in the old integration variables at fixed exterior. It does not assert strict locality of a minimizing-field response when the exterior itself varies. Prior product representation and influence estimate · Rooted bound and its domain

Let \(F_1(\xi_1,e),G_2(\xi_2,e)\) have envelopes \(B_1,B_2\). An optional selector \(A\) may depend on \(\xi_1,e\). For \(\delta\le1\) the true two-core density divided by the product of its **true marginals** lies in \([e^{-6\delta},e^{6\delta}]\), by applying the product-reference bound to the joint density and each marginal. Subtracting that product inside the covariance gains \(C\delta\). For \(\delta>1\) simply bound the covariance by \(2 B_1B_2\Pi(A\mid e)\). Thus, including its exact posterior selector,

\[
|\operatorname{Cov}({\bf1}_A F_1,G_2\mid e)|
\le C B_1B_2\,\Pi(A\mid e)\min(1,\delta). \tag{N.13}
\]

One application only needs an outer geometric selection. Use the §5 restart and a cut \(k\) with \(k-N(k)\le J\le k\) and \(k-J\le C_0(1+R_J)\), for fixed \(C_0\). Keep the (1.103) fibers with one body containing the fixed root \(x\), and another containing \(y\). On every such fiber eligibility before the final split forbids new births in the selected body's preceding \(N(k)\) steps. An occupied \(J\)-cell must then occur within \(C s_k\) of \(x\), by tracing covers and bounded fills back to \(J\). A fixed enlargement covers the diameter of the eligible body. The number of possible \(J\)-cells is at most \(\exp[C(1+k-J)]\) by H8.3. The actual probability of this exterior event is therefore at most \(\exp[-q_0+C(1+k-J)]\), regardless of the other core, with \(q_0=c\Gamma_*\).

Both eligible bodies have diameter at most \(100MR_k\) in current units, and exterior boundary size at most \(C(1+R_k)^4\). Thus \(\delta\le C(1+R_k)^4e^{C R_k}e^{-c r/h_k}\), for root separation \(r\). Apply (N.13) with \(A\) the entire fiber, and take the actual posterior-weighted outer expectation over the selected exteriors. For H9.29 microscopic envelopes, \(q_0\gg J+R_J+R_0^4\) pays the entropy, the body factors and the two-mark age through \(k\). Summing \(h_k^{-8}T(h_k)^{2s}e^{-c r/h_k}\) on this band proves a bound \(C_s A_x^{\rm env}A_y^{\rm env}e^{-c'q_0}r^{-8}T(r)^{2s}\) for these selected *conditional* covariances. The two marks must be actual functions of their respective live variables and \(e\), with the stated envelope. An old numerator already integrated into a nonlocal function of \(e\) is outside this calculation. In the law of total covariance the covariance of the two \(e\)-conditional means is still present.

There is a complementary range requiring no early-ancestry condition. On every such two-body fiber with
\[
r/h_k\ge C_s(1+k+R_k),
\]
use the distance exponent itself to pay \(D_s k\), \(C R_k\) and the boundary-size prefactor, keeping a fixed fraction of the exponential. The same scale sum gives \(C_s A_x^{\rm env}A_y^{\rm env}r^{-8}T(r)^{2s}\) for the within-fiber contribution on these cuts, after integration over any other fixed live cores and exteriors with their posterior weights. Each cut uses the pair selected by the two root homes, so there is no unrestricted count of pairs. This still describes the conditional covariance at the level where the other live cores are fixed; covariance created by averaging their means remains an additional term.

Other live cores can be averaged on two further classes. On the real separated-body class of T1 §7.80, diameters are bounded by \(C R_k\) and gaps below by \(a_0R_k\), with \(a_0>0\) fixed. The center distance of two bodies is then at most \((1+2C/a_0)\) times their boundary distance. Reserving part of the joining tree length in T1 (7.201) gives a Dobrushin influence row sum below \(1/2\) with weight \(e^{h d_M(c_i,c_j)}\) for a small fixed \(h>0\) in native units. The comparison of one-site conditional laws, with all other sites integrated, bounds the oscillation of \(\mathbb E[G_2\mid \xi_1,e]\) by \(C B_2 e^{-h d_M(c_1,c_2)}\). The \(L^1\) factor \(\mathbb E[{\bf1}_A |F_1|\mid e]\) again retains the full posterior probability.

On the early band above, simultaneous eligibility of \(m\) distinct prescribed live shapes forces a fixed fraction of \(m\) distinct \(J\)-ancestors, after coloring the enlarged neighborhoods of their canonical \(k\)-cell anchors. The H9.25 joint bound and the trace-back entropy give a cost \(e^{-c q_0 m}\). Under the actual connected-carrier hypotheses of T1 Lemma 7.194 and Proposition 7.195 this pays \(e^{C\sum_i |Y_i|_M}\) for the bodies incident to a connected contribution, even without the separated-body assumption. Each eligible rectangle has at most \(C R_k^4\) native cells, and has a bounded number of possible shapes per \(MR_k\)-anchor. For a connected support of \(n\) native cells the sum over anchor subsets costs at most \((1+C e^{-c'q_0})^{C n}\) after reserving a fixed fraction of the rarity. The spare native tree rate absorbs it. The resulting \(r^{-8}T(r)^{2s}\) bound on this band includes the mediating live integrations of the real (1.103) law. It still conditions on the remaining exterior. Fixed-native-distance comparison and connected-carrier count

### 10. Combining birth layers without a conditional product

At an observation cut \(d\), color a specified family of distinct cells to retain \(n\) cells with disjoint enlarged witness neighborhoods, a fixed fraction of the original family. Consider a deterministic list of candidate origin layers \(k\). For cell \(i\) let \(B_{ki}\) require an actual witness in its neighborhood at layer \(k\), **including** the carrier restriction of the witness theorem. Suppose, for every subset \(I\) and each layer separately,

\[
\Pi\left(\bigcap_{i\in I}B_{ki}\right)\le e^{-s_k|I|},\qquad s_k>0.
\]

Examples include the specified rare base, RB9.21 with its strict global carrier, and a family of predictable gauge or reset innovations whose address multiplicities have already been paid. For strict physical flags, a test at step \(k\) in a radius \(C s_d^{\rm cell}\) neighborhood of a cut-\(d\) cell costs at most \(\exp[C(1+d-k+\log(1+R_{\max}))]\) choices, where \(s_d^{\rm cell}=Mh_dR_d\). This yields \(s_k=c p_k^2-C(1+d-k+\log(1+R_{\max}))\) with \(p_k=p_0(g_k)\). Disjoint neighborhoods ensure distinct cubes. This use of RB9.21 averages each layer over its own actual law without imposing a past or future label from any other layer.

Set \(q_{\rm eff}=(\sum_k s_k^{-1})^{-1}\). The probability that **every** one of the \(n\) cells has at least one of these witnesses is at most

\[
\exp[-(q_{\rm eff}-\log 2)n]. \tag{N.14}
\]

Indeed \(N_k=\sum_i{\bf1}_{B_{ki}}\) has \(\mathbb E e^{s_k N_k}\le(1+(e^{s_k}-1)e^{-s_k})^n\le 2^n\), by expanding the finite product. Hölder with weights \(q_{\rm eff}/s_k\) gives \(\mathbb E e^{q_{\rm eff}\sum N_k}\le 2^n\). All claimed cells require \(\sum N_k\ge n\). No cross-layer conditional independence or screening enters this estimate.

For a window of \(D\) layers with comparable raw rates bounded below by \(q_*\), the above entropy leaves \(q_{\rm eff}\ge c q_*/D\) if \(1+D+\log(1+R_{\max})\) is a sufficiently small multiple of \(q_*\). In particular \(D\le c_\lambda q_* /(1+R_{\max}^4)\) can leave the ordinary-size reserve \(\lambda R_d^4\). To pay the age of a bare microscopic pair at the same cut requires the separate inequality \(q_{\rm eff}\ge C_s(1+d)\). The cell event can be applied directly as a spatial selection, allowing arbitrary intervening processing. An actual ancestry qualifies if each selected cell traces to such a witness within the stated neighborhood. A replacement or failure can be traced to a previous occupied cell when its source operation is supported there; one must follow cells rather than jump across an arbitrarily large joined component. A canonical-large birth in an unadmitted mixed parent needs its own witness. The harmonic cost in (N.14) accounts explicitly for the loss when only separate layer bounds have been certified.

When the geometry gives a **common** last globally strict entry in a deterministic list of \(D\) cuts, the rate can be higher. Require that from this entry every selected cell has a cone witness at the entry or in a later family of predictable innovations; later redirections must terminate at one of these origins. For each possible last cut, discard the lastness condition for an upper bound, apply the chronological estimate of §4 to the fixed entry and later innovations, and sum over the \(D\) cuts. If the within-cone address entropy is paid, this costs \(\log D\) for the whole family and leaves a fixed fraction of \(q_*\) per retained cell. The common-cut condition matters: (N.14) remains available for certified spatial events that do not share such a cut.

### 11. Translation averages of terminal survivors

Use the original stopped normalization of T1 §7.92, including the lower bound \(Z_{\rm stop}\ge e^{-C_-V_K}\) for the same vacuum subtraction and admissible terminal torus. Here \(V_K\) counts the volume in terminal lattice units up to a fixed convention factor. In T1 (7.212) take the list to contain every eligible shape and position at a fixed scale \(k\). This list is finite; each realized body still live in an unbroken terminal lineage is counted once by \(N_\infty\), and the right side of (7.212) has no list-length factor. Consequently

\[
\mathbb E e^{c p_k^2 N_\infty}\le e^{C V_K},\qquad
\mathbb E N_\infty\le C V_K/p_k^2,\qquad
\|N_\infty\|_2\le C(V_K+1)/p_k^2 .
\]

The first moment follows by Jensen. The second follows by integrating the tail \(\min(1,\exp(CV_K-cp_k^2u))\) in \(u\). An eligible shape at cut \(k\) covers at most \(C R_k^4\) native home sites. Uniform translation of a microscopic root samples these sites with their exact equal multiplicities on the compatible torus. There are \(V_k=L^{4(K-k)}V_K\) sites in the same volume convention. Hence for the event \(S_x\) that the home of \(x\) at \(k\) lies in one of these surviving bodies,

\[
\operatorname{Av}_z\Pi(S_{x+z})
\le C R_k^4p_k^{-2} L^{-4(K-k)}. \tag{N.15}
\]

For an actual old mark with *incoming* deterministic envelope \(A h_k^{-4}\) on the specified homes, (N.15) gives an \(L^1\) bound \(C A\ell_0^{-4}R_k^4/p_k^2\) on the translated average of its absolute terminal selected numerator. Conditional Jensen gives the same bound after projection to the terminal state, including when paired with a terminal test of supremum norm one. Taking the spatial average **before squaring** also gives this \(L^2\) bound, up to a fixed factor. Pathwise, the absolute translated average is at most \(C A h_k^{-4}R_k^4 N_\infty/V_k\); then apply the displayed second moment and conditional Jensen. A logarithmic factor in the incoming envelope is carried through. An original mark known only at scale \(a\) retains its incoming \(a^{-4}\) cost.

The variance in V9.7 squares each translated point field before averaging. A count bound alone cannot interchange these operations. For example, select one uniformly random fine home among \(m\) homes, record its index in the output, and let the \(m\) marks be \(B\) times their corresponding selection indicators. The squared spatial mean is \(B^2/m^2\), while the mean of individual terminal variances is \(B^2(1-1/m)/m\). Two replicas conditional on the output have the same recorded index. This illustrates the missing estimate on how the physical terminal state distinguishes the incoming root positions. Stopped normalization and survivor multiplicities

There is a summable consequence for a family of actual incoming remainders whose envelope at scale \(k\) is \(C g_k^2 h_k^{-4}\) on these homes. For a compact test \(f\) on the common physical torus, smear with the usual discrete volume weight \(h_k^4\). Translate the home assignment by the common uniform microscopic shift, using a fixed block-compatible projection to the \(k\)-grid. The triangle inequality and (N.15) bound its translated mean absolute selected contribution by \(C\ell_0^{-4}\|f\|_{1,k}\,g_k^2 R_k^4/p_k^2\), where \(\|f\|_{1,k}=h_k^4\sum_{y\in\Lambda_k}|f(y)|\). This bound does not grow with the physical volume for a fixed compact test. Under the standing positive inverse-coupling drift, \(x_k\asymp x_K+K-k\) on a fixed stopped interval. With \(p_k\asymp(1+\log x_k)^P\), the sum over \(K-k\ge m\) is bounded by a constant times

\[
\sup_k\|f\|_{1,k}
\sum_{n\ge m}\frac{1}{(x_K+n)(1+\log(x_K+n))^{2P-4r}}.
\]

This tends to zero uniformly on the fixed stopped weak interval. Thus the early-scale tail of this **already matched** one-source family vanishes in translated \(L^1\) even for unbroken terminal survivors. The statement uses the actual \(g_k^2\) gain in the incoming envelope wherever it is applied. It does not establish that gain for the missing microscopic scalar transport, nor imply convergence of squared or higher local products.

**A separate cofinal estimate for very old terminal layers.** Under the same stopped inputs take a compatible sequence with \(V_K=o(K)\), and fix \(0<\delta<1\). T1 (7.216) bounds the actual probability of a prescribed home lying in terminal determining layer \(\Omega_n\setminus\Omega_{n+1}\), \(n<K\), by \(C e^{C_A V_K}L^{-A(K-n-1)}\), for a fixed \(A\) on its weak margin. For \(n\le(1-\delta)K\) sum over \(n\) and choose \(A\delta>8\) with fixed slack. The factors \(K+1\), the logarithm in the bare microscopic envelope and the cost \(L^{8K}\) in §§1–2 are then absorbed with an exponentially small remainder. This bounds both the selected terminal variance change and the cutwise covariance costs in (N.1) on the same marked lift for these long-lived terminal layers. No clean or origin-type restriction on those actual terminal layers is required.

For example the cubic candidates of §1.3 admit terminal side \(2L^{m_0+\lfloor(\log_L K)/8\rfloor}\) in terminal units, giving \(V_K=O(K^{1/2})\) and growing physical side. The native tiling/size conditions and the actual trajectory must hold on the proposed sequence. This is a separate diagonal from the explicit O0/N0 choice with terminal-side exponent at least \(K\). It also fails the easy sufficient condition \(\log(1/a)/T\to0\) in §2.15 and Annex M. Using this diagonal for the full existence assembly would require the base and operator inputs to be established together on it. The diagonal result does not supply a volume-uniform localized partition ratio.

### 12. A terminal scalar contribution and a test for the missing variance

Fix one microscopic home. On the healing records for which its full plaquette stencil still consists of literal compact integration variables in one body, insert the once-owned affine factor \(1+tF_0\) in that body's original integral. Put any common deterministic subtraction outside the sum of records once. The compact word bound \(B_0\) gives a source radius \(c/B_0\) in the exterior chart. T1 (7.193) then bounds the new proper disjoint \(R\) coefficient by \(CB_0e^{-p_j}\) in its rooted norm. With the H9 physical entry bound, keep origins satisfying \(4j\log L\le p_j/4\), and at subsequent promotions \(j\to e\) keep \(4(e-j)\log L\le p_e/4\). The factor \(e^{-p_e}\) in (7.254) pays the rescaling and the chart cost at each such promotion. In dimensionless units the resulting coefficients obey \(d_e\le C e^{-p_e/2}(1+\sum_{j<e}d_j)\). The sum of \(e^{-p_e/2}\) is bounded on the stopped weak trajectories in question.

For a resulting direct coefficient, T1's pointwise final-layer contraction gives the factor \(L^{-4(K-e)}\) up to chart and age polynomials. On lower layers keep the term only if its responsible incident terminal cell belongs to a specified class of probability at most \(C e^{-\rho}\) per location. The indicator then has \(L^2\) norm at most \(C e^{-\rho/2}\). The rooted exponential support norm pays for the finite support collar. Taking \(\rho\ge8(K-e)\log L\) pays dimension four for these lower-layer terms as well. This gives a volume-uniform terminal \(L^2\) estimate for the selected direct scalar terms, including repeated proper promotions. The bounds leave carried exterior terms, other promotions and unselected terminal incidences in the complementary field. Entry, promotion and terminal estimates, §§15–17

For the full field there is an exact finite-cutoff diagnostic. Tilt the microscopic law by \(\exp(\sum_{z\in Z}t_z F_{0,z})\), normalize it, and use the original zero-source kernels. The score of the physical terminal marginal at \(t=0\) is \(E(F_{0,z}\mid U_K)-EF_{0,z}\), by differentiating the finite integral. Consequently the trace of its Fisher information divided by \(|Z|\) equals the translation average of the individual terminal variances in V9.7. A constant tilt tests one quadratic form of that matrix. The trace requires control of all its modes. Equivalently it is an averaged overlap of two centered microscopic marks drawn independently conditional on the same physical terminal state. The exterior average of this overlap is still unbounded at the required scale by the present arguments.

### 13. A stopped trajectory from signed windows

Consider O0's actual causal recursion \(x_{i+1}=x_i-d_i+r_i\), with the certified bound \(|r_i|\le C x_i^{-1/8}(1+\log x_i)^6\) on its admitted weak domain. Assume the outstanding signed Gaussian window inequality
\[
 d_-(b-a)-B\le\sum_{a\le i<b}d_i\le d_+(b-a)+B,\qquad d_->0. \tag{N.16}
\]
Use the native continuation domain of O0 §§10–10.2, including its interscale hypotheses. Choose \(X\) so large that the error on the weak interval is at most \(d_-/2\) and a single step of size at most \(D\) leaves \(X-D\) inside that interval. Start at arbitrarily large \(x_0\), and stop at the first \(K\) with \(x_K\le X\). If this never occurred, the lower window from zero would force \(x_n\le x_0+B-nd_-/2\), a contradiction. The last step gives \(X-D\le x_K\le X\), and bounded steps give \(K\ge(x_0-X)/D\). Compactness now gives a subsequence with a common limiting weak endpoint. This construction requires estimates uniform on its compact terminal interval. It supplies neither a prescribed exact endpoint at each depth nor a proof of (N.16). O0 value estimate and admission domain

If additionally \(\sum_{a\le i<b}(d_i-d)=O(1)\) on every interval with \(d>0\), then the physical recursion implies
\[
 d\sum_{a\le i<b} x_i^{-1}=\log(x_a/x_b)
   +O\!\left(x_b^{-1}+x_b^{-1/8}(1+\log x_b)^6\right). \tag{N.17}
\]
Indeed the window bounds give \(x_i\asymp x_b+b-i\) on \([a,b]\) for sufficiently weak stop, and bounded steps give \(|1/x_{i+1}-1/x_i|\le C/x_i^2\). Expand the log ratio over one step. The quadratic Taylor errors sum to \(O(1/x_b)\). Summation by parts bounds \(\sum(d_i-d)/x_i\) by the endpoints and the total variation of \(1/x_i\), also \(O(1/x_b)\). Summing the stated physical error divided by \(x_i\) gives the other term. Thus individual sign changes in \(d_i\) are compatible with the interval powers used in the regular mean calculation.

For an **exact prescribed** weak terminal value at each depth, O0 Theorem 10.1 uses continuity along the actual bare-parameter axis. On the matched fixed physical cutoff this can be checked at each finite depth. In unscaled link variables the normalized positive integrals CMP109 (0.15)–(0.19) have the same Haar readouts and supports for nearby admitted parameter histories. The cutoff (2.9) is on the physical independent coordinates relative to the common classical axial saddle. Before redilating the fluctuations, the exponents that vary in the iterated integral are the bare Wilson action and the soft gauge actions with their respective coefficients. At fixed depth their absolute values per terminal site are bounded by a depth-dependent constant on compact link space. Comparing histories at distance \(\delta\) in the maximum norm changes the normalized log integral per site by at most \(C_j\delta\). The earlier full action, evaluated at the same registered minimizing background in (1.6), obeys the corresponding estimate at the preceding depth. O0's uniform real fourth-derivative bound for the two external plane-wave amplitudes gives a Hessian modulus \(O_j(\sqrt{\delta})\) by finite differences. Fourier inversion and the uniform exponential tail (12.10) give a modulus \(O_j(\sqrt{\delta}(1+|\log\delta|)^6)\) for the native second moment. Induction gives the finite-depth continuity needed for shooting. The variation is taken along admitted histories. This verification applies to the specified physical cutoff. Fixed-cutoff path comparison and source locations, §13.1

The compact weak stop bounds the terminal-unit native divisibility requirement uniformly, since the scale-\((K-i)\) partition radius grows at most as a fixed power of \(\log(C+i)\) while the unit conversion contributes \(L^{-i}\). This permits the arithmetic of §11's smaller tori. For the operator step on those same tori one sufficient replacement for Annex M's \(K/T\to0\) is the conjunction of its actual one-field heat-sandwich estimate, \(G=\limsup_n\Gamma_n/T_n<\infty\), and convergent hierarchies with locally bounded limiting moment functions off spacetime diagonals. At strictly separated times multiple insertions factor into individually heat-sandwiched fields. Log convexity in Annex M (4.10) gives \(\limsup_n q_{F,n}(t_n)\le e^{4Gt}p(F)^2\) also for null tests, so the constant of the reduced inequality changes by a bounded factor for \(0<t\le1\). On compact sets away from spacetime coincidences the equal-time strips have vanishing limiting contribution by dominated convergence in both reflected forms. The individual slab, trace and hierarchy bounds on this sequence remain hypotheses. Interval estimates and periodic-state extension, §13

### 14. The unmarked coarea factor and the scalar magnetic finite part

**Gaussian density on a quotient.** Let \(Q:H_f\to H_c\) be an onto map between finite-dimensional Euclidean physical spaces. Choose an orthonormal inclusion \(I\) of its kernel and \(R=Q^*(QQ^*)^{-1}\). For a symmetric precision \(M\), suppose \(A=I^*MI>0\), and let \(M'\) be the Schur complement in coordinates \((I,R)\). For \(s>0\) with \(M'+sI>0\),
\[
 [\det(QQ^*)\det A]^{-1/2}
 =\left[\frac{\det(M'+sI)}{\det(M+sQ^*Q)}\right]^{1/2}. \tag{N.18}
\]
Indeed \(|\det(I,R)|=(\det QQ^*)^{-1/2}\), by orthogonality. Taking determinants of the two-block matrix proves (N.18). The left side is precisely the coarea density times the fiber Gaussian determinant, up to a field-independent Gaussian constant. This equality holds for every allowed background before any differentiation. If a fine or coarse physical quotient carries orbit-volume density \(J_f\) or \(J_c\) relative to orthogonal volume, the ratio \(J_f/J_c\) remains in the pushforward. For a locally free, isometric gauge action with Haar parameters and infinitesimal action \(D\), the linear orbit density is \(\sqrt{\det D^*D}\) in those parameter coordinates. Roots, stabilizers, and volume forms must be matched when using the formula in a gauge chart. T9.46's differentiation in a tensor source eliminates more factors than an unmarked curvature derivative does. T9, §12.1

**One scalar determinant in infinite volume.** On the unit four-dimensional lattice take the nearest-neighbor covariant scalar Laplacian with hopping rate one to each neighbor, unit Abelian charge, and constant flux \(F\) in the 12-plane. Define \(s(F)-s(0)\) as its log determinant per site relative to zero field, by the bulk heat trace integral. There is a finite real \(b\) for which
\[
 s(F)-s(0)=\frac{F^2}{96\pi^2}\log\frac1{|F|}+bF^2+o(F^2).
 \tag{N.19}
\]
The normalization refers to one scalar eigenvalue per site. Other charges replace \(F\) by their charge times \(F\). This calculation uses the original nearest-neighbor scalar operator. It does not identify the full constrained vector determinant in O0 (13.14).

To prove (N.19), expand the heat semigroup in continuous-time nearest-neighbor walks. On a closed walk the phase of the parallel transport is \(F A\), with signed lattice area \(A=\int X\,dY\) in the first two coordinates. Reflection drops the imaginary part. With \(p_t=P(S_t=0)\) and \(E_t\) the return bridge expectation, the relative log density is
\[
 \int_0^\infty p_t\, E_t[1-\cos(FA)]\,\frac{dt}{t}.
\]
Condition on the jump counts \(n_x,n_y\) of the two coordinate bridges. On rescaling time to \([0,1]\), the positive and negative jump times in each coordinate are two independent collections of \(n/2\) uniform variables. Thus \(E_y(A^2\mid X,n_y)=n_y[\int X^2-(\int X)^2]\). The covariance of \(X(u),X(v)\) at fixed \(n_x\) equals \(n_x(\min(u,v)-uv)\). Integration gives \(E(A^2\mid n_x,n_y)=n_x n_y/12\). Pairing positive and negative jump times expresses \(A\) as a sum of independent centered differences conditional on \(X,n_y\), with fourth moment at most \(C n_y^2\int X^4\). Doing the same for each \(X(u)\) bounds this last integral in expectation by \(C n_x^2\).

In a one-coordinate bridge the count \(n=2h\) has probabilities proportional to \(t^{2h}/(h!)^2\), so \(E_t n^2=4t^2\) by shifting \(h\), and \(E_t n=2t I_1(2t)/I_0(2t)=2t+O(1)\) as \(t\to\infty\). Here \(I_\nu\) are the modified Bessel functions. The same elementary Laplace expansion of the one-dimensional return integral gives \(p_t=(e^{-2t}I_0(2t))^4=(4\pi t)^{-2}(1+O(t^{-1}))\). Independence of the coordinates now gives \(p_t E_t A^2=1/(48\pi^2)+O(t^{-1})\) for \(t\ge1\) and \(E_t A^4\le Ct^4\) for all \(t\). At small \(t\), \(E_t n=O(t^2)\), so the quadratic moment is integrable against \(dt/t\).

Separate the term \(F^2p_t E_t A^2/2\) for \(t\le1/|F|\). Its integral yields the displayed log and a finite multiple of \(F^2\) with error \(o(F^2)\). In the remainder put \(u=t|F|\) and divide by \(F^2\). The density with respect to \(du\) is bounded in absolute value by \(Cu\) on \(0<u\le1\) and \(Cu^{-3}\) on \(u>1\), using the fourth moment, \(1-\cos v\le2\) and \(p_t\le Ct^{-2}\). The integrand converges at each \(u>0\). Indeed the bridge area divided by \(t\) has a limit in law. For a partition into \(m\) equal intervals replace \(X\) in \(\int X\,dY\) by its left-endpoint values. The conditional second-moment formula bounds the mean square error by \(n_xn_y/m\), averaged over the two fixed-count bridges. After division by \(t^2\) this is \(O(1/m)\) uniformly for \(t\ge1\). For each fixed partition the values of \(X/\sqrt t,Y/\sqrt t\) converge jointly to those of independent Brownian bridges of diffusion coefficient two, by the finite-dimensional central limit theorem for the uniform jump times and \(n/t\to2\) in probability. Their left-endpoint area sums converge in mean square by the same increment estimate. Dominated convergence in \(u\) proves existence of the stated finite part.

In coarse lattice units the fine scalar log density at the same physical flux is \(L^4[s(F/L^2)-s(0)]\). Subtracting \(s(F)-s(0)\) cancels \(b\) and yields \(F^2\log L/(48\pi^2)+o(F^2)\). T1 (7.244) already gives the bridge second moment. The fourth-moment estimate and the rescaled bridge limit above give the finite part in the massless bulk log density. This identifies the scalar scale difference without differentiating a quantized-flux parameter on a fixed torus. A native trajectory estimate still requires the signed combination with the vector Hessian, the actual gauge and readout, and identification with the infinite-volume polarization of CMP109 (1.22). T1, §§7.114–7.122

### 15. The signed Gaussian window in the matched fixed-cutoff prescription

Fix \(SU(N)\), an admitted large odd \(L\), and the regular Gaussian endpoint of O0 (12.13) for CMP109 (0.10), (0.12), (2.9). The following deduction uses the uniform regular tangent, constrained Green and localized Gaussian estimates specified below and the Wilson heat estimates of T1 §§7.113–7.122. These are substantive inputs to the proof. With \(d_j\) the actual projected Gaussian decrement in the program variable \(x=X_B/(2N^2)\), they give
\[
 \left|\sum_{j<k}d_j-\frac{11\log L}{24\pi^2}\,k\right|\le C_{N,L,R}
 \quad\text{for every } k. \tag{N.20}
\]
Here \(R\) is one sufficiently large fixed compatible terminal period used to measure flux. It is independent of \(k\) and of the volume on which the physical trajectory is later used.

**Extraction of the native coefficient.** Use T1 Lemma 7.187's genuine periodic flux \(q_R T\) in a single plane on the period \(R\) and zero flux on the same period. Choose \(T\) regular integral Cartan and \(q_R=2\pi/R^2\) small enough for the chart and distinct charged eigenphases. Repeat each field on larger compatible covers. The opposite Wilson forces cancel and blocking reproduces affine curvature, so the fine stationary fields have scale-\(j\) flux \(q_j=q_R L^{-2(k-j)}\). The constrained Hessian here is the stationary Wilson Hessian. Its multiplier times the second derivative of the constraint vanishes at these specific fields. Gaussian integration in stages, including the densities of each Haar readout, gives the cumulative hard determinant of the fine Wilson action. Its increment is the new Gaussian term of CMP109 (1.6) after subtraction of the previous action at the new minimum. An earlier quadratic curvature contributes its old coefficient under this substitution by affine reproduction. N2 Appendix H.1 · O0 §12.5

The *unmarked* new Gaussian log has the native local expansion (1.7), with (1.18)–(1.19)'s exponential weight, complex chart and local gauge invariance. On a lifted nonwinding support of diameter \(D\), the centered affine Cartan potential of flux \(q\) has normalized potential \(O((1+D)|q|)\), derivatives \(O(|q|)\) and current zero. On \(|q|\le c/(1+D)\) it obeys the strict small bounds that imply the synthetic chart condition before (1.18). Cauchy's estimate for \(D\le |q_j|^{-1/2}\) bounds the cubic remainder by \(C|q_j|^3(1+D)^3\) times its support weight. Larger supports have a summable exponential tail both at the true real regular field and for the zero-field jets. Their contribution obeys the same cubic bound after summation. The Gaussian vacuum cancels in the flux difference and the linear term vanishes by color invariance.

For clarity, the quadratic term is the infinite-lattice projection in CMP109 (1.22). At fixed depth test its Hessian with two periodic transverse waves, \(\sin(p x_1)/p,\cos(p x_1)/p\) in direction 2 and color \(T\). The Hessian is taken at zero amplitude. On a bounded lifted carrier a locally constant potential can be gauged away. The remaining linear slopes of these waves at its home are \(\cos(p x_1),-\sin(p x_1)\). Their squares add to one. The error in replacing the waves by their affine tangents tends to zero after the localized tangent and support sums, by O0 (12.8)–(12.10). At fixed depth take growing covers first, then \(p\to0\). The gauge identity and lattice symmetry give leading symbol \(c(|p|^2\delta_{\mu\nu}-p_\mu p_\nu)\) for a unit color, so the two-wave test recovers \(c=-\partial_{p_1}\partial_{p_2}\widehat P_{12}(0)\). This identifies the local affine quadratic term by a summed periodic test, including possible total divergences. The normalized cubic error in measuring the whole prefix is \(O_R(\sum_{j\le k}|q_j|)=O_R(1)\) because the number of earlier sites cancels two powers of \(q_j/q_R\). Winding terms vanish on the covers at fixed depth. Unmarked chart, paired waves and order of limits, §14

**The Haar density and the low eigenvalues.** In raw orthonormal link coordinates let \(d_f,d_c\) be the gauge differentials and let \(E\) evaluate gauge parameters at the terminal centers. The readout intertwines these maps. Write \(W_i=\ker d_i\), \(\Delta_i=d_i^*d_i\), \(\overline Q\) for the map of physical quotients, \(H_f,H_c\) for the physical Hessians, and \(h=L^{-k}\). Equation (N.18) and normalized integration over the internal group fixing the centers give the finite-dimensional hard density, up to background-independent constants,
\[
 |\det(E:W_f\to W_c)|
 \left(\frac{\det'\Delta_f}{\det'\Delta_c}\right)^{1/2}
 \left(\frac{\det(H_c+\sigma I)}
 {\det(H_f+\sigma\overline Q^*\overline Q)}\right)^{1/2}. \tag{N.21}
\]
To see the first factor, split a link volume into physical volume and gauge-orbit volume, the latter being \((\det'\Delta)^{1/2}\) times site-parameter volume divided by stabilizer volume. Fine site parameters split orthogonally into those vanishing on centers and the retained parameters. The density on the remaining stabilizer therefore changes by the determinant of its center restriction. The computation is a change of tangent volumes of the free internal gauge action. On either stationary field the stabilizers are parallel sections with the same pointwise norm at all sites, and their restriction is an isomorphism, so this determinant is \(h^{2r}\), \(r=\dim W_f=\dim W_c\) at that field.

The actual composite readout and tangent extension \(M\) have \(\|\overline Q\|\le Ch,\ \|M\|\le C/h,\ \overline Q M=I\). The first bound is N2 Appendix I.2 after conversion from physical connection norm to raw link norm. For the second, CMP102 §G (172)–(173) and Proposition 9 fix one Landau representative of the entire relative configuration at the given regular base field. Its return to the axial representative is a gauge transformation, so differentiating at zero gives the same physical tangent class. In this representative a normalized coarse variation changes a fine link by \(O(h)\) times its exponentially decaying response in terminal units, by CMP102 Proposition 9, (190), as used at the stationary field in T1 Lemma 7.187. Squaring and counting \(h^{-4}\) links per terminal cell, or applying the two weighted row and column bounds, gives \(C/h\) in operator norm uniformly on the covers. Projection to a physical representative decreases that norm. T1 Lemma 7.188 gives \(H_f|_{\ker\overline Q}\ge c h^2\), while \(\|H_c\|\le C\) by the regular extension and the stationary plaquette Hessian. For sufficiently large fixed \(\sigma\), write a vector as \(t+Mb\) with \(\overline Q t=0\). Stationarity kills the cross term and gives \(H_f+\sigma\overline Q^*\overline Q\ge c' h^2 I\).

The Wilson form bound and the scalar heat return at \(s=h^{-2}\) in T1 Lemma 7.188 bound the dimension of the physical spectral subspace below \(c_1h^2\) by \(C_R\) times the terminal volume \(V\). Stabilize it by \(C_1h^2P_{\rm low}\). This stabilized positive matrix and the preceding softened hard matrix differ by rank \(O_R(V)\) and norm \(O(h^2)\). Along their convex interpolation the inverse norm is \(O(h^{-2})\), so integrating the logarithmic derivative costs \(O_R(V)\). The coarse vector factor of (N.21) has bounded log per cell. The coarse nonzero ghost logarithm has bounded bulk density, by the ordinary Fourier integral at zero flux and on neutral colors, and by the magnetic gap on charged colors at nonzero flux.

Adding \(d_fd_f^*\) on the longitudinal space converts the stabilized physical vector determinant to the full Feynman vector determinant \(\det L_{\rm s}\), with longitudinal factor \(\det'\Delta_f\). The two fine gauge factors together give a full ghost. In the log density the stabilizer term \(2r\log h\) combines with \(\log\det'\Delta_f\) to give \(\log\det(\Delta_f+h^2P_{W_f})\) exactly. Thus a jump in stabilizer dimension at zero flux adds no spurious slope. The remaining fine log density is the stabilized ghost log determinant minus half the stabilized full vector log determinant, to \(O_R(V)\). Tangent-volume and finite-rank comparison, §§1–3.1

**Sign and normalization.** Use the fixed rescaling of the Wilson quadratic form for which the flat kinetic operator is the unit lattice Laplacian, as in T1 (7.245). A fixed kinetic normalization cancels between the flux and vacuum up to at most a fixed-rank constant, whose density vanishes on the covers. In fine units the charged plaquette phase is \(B=h^2q_R\alpha(T)\) for root \(\alpha\). T1 (7.245), before any metric derivative, gives the \(B^2\) coefficient of the half-vector-minus-ghost heat trace per charged mode as \(11/[6(4\pi)^2]+O(s^{-1/2})\) on \(1\le s\le h^{-2}\). Its sign in the log *density* is positive because the vector log determinant there has negative half weight. T1 Lemma 7.189 bounds the finite-flux remainder by \(Cs^{-2}(|B|s)^3\). The quadratic error and the cubic remainder both integrate to \(O_R(1)\) per terminal cell with \(ds/s\). The finite-rank modifications cost \(O_R(V)sh^2e^{C_Rsh^2}\) in the heat trace on this interval. The short-time flux difference is \(O(B^2s)\) per fine site, and the long-time stabilized physical and charged-ghost tails cost \(O_R(1)\) per terminal cell, by the gap and trace at \(h^{-2}\). The neutral nonzero scalar modes common to both fluxes cancel or have the ordinary integrable bulk tail. For regular \(T\), neutral Cartan link variations commute with the background. Their compact Wilson curl form changes by a relative \(O(h^4q_R^2)\) on the varying plane from the cosine weight, costing \(O_R(1)\) per terminal cell. These estimates allow the growing-cover limit first for each \(h\). Wilson heat, finite-flux and endpoint estimates

For \(\operatorname{Tr}T^2=1/2\) the sum of squared adjoint charges is \(N\), by summing \((T_{ii}-T_{jj})^2\) over \(i,j\). The calculation with integral \(T\) scales to this generator by the fixed quadratic norm when extracting the coefficient. The logarithmic heat window is \(2k\log L\), giving \(11N k\log L/(48\pi^2)\) as the coefficient of the one-plane squared flux in the log density. The normalized color form after CMP109 (4.33) is \(\operatorname{tr}AB=\operatorname{Tr}(AB)/N\). The mixed momentum projection of \(cF^2\) tested on this generator is \(2c\), so \(\beta^G=4Nc\), with program decrement \(2c/N\). This proves (N.20) with a bounded endpoint error. The continuum background-field calculation gives a separate check on this normalization. [CMP109, pp.264 and 289](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01215223) · [Abbott, background-field calculation](https://www.columbia.edu/cu/neurotheory/Larry/AbbottNP81a.pdf)

Subtract (N.20) at two depths to obtain (N.16) with common slope \(d=11\log L/(24\pi^2)\) and bounded additive error. With the value modulus and causal construction of O0, §13 supplies exact weak-endpoint shooting in this matched prescription. In particular \(x_j\asymp x_K+K-j\) and \(x_0=dK+O((1+K)^{7/8}(1+\log(1+K))^6)\). The sharper per-step law and coupling derivative in N2-F do not follow. The new deduction is relative to the stated native source and source estimates, especially T1's heat comparison at the stationary flux. It leaves the complementary S2 conditional covariance, terminal scalar variance, and reconstruction hypotheses open.

### 16. Accumulated occupied time at a fixed physical home

Use the normalized positive T1 joint law on the compatible tori of §§11, 13 and 15. Fix a physical home \(x\) with a deterministic boundary convention. Count a pre-replacement cut when its genuine occupied component contains \(x\) and the stored determining sets retain this point along its unbroken succession. Assign that cut to the first subsequent complete healing of that component, or to its surviving terminal body. A later fresh episode at the same point is counted on its own succession. Each cut is counted at most once. Write \(H_{\rm ret}(x)\) for the number assigned to healings and \(H_{\rm live}(x)\) for the remaining count. Old source coefficients already emitted to the exterior acquire no occupied-time credit unless the home again meets the stated test.

For every fixed \(A>0\) with the corresponding weak margin, the same law satisfies
\[
 \begin{split}
 E e^{A H_{\rm ret}(x)} &\le \exp\!\left(C_A\sum_{\ell\le K} e^{-p_\ell}\right),\\
 E e^{A(H_{\rm ret}+H_{\rm live})(x)}
 &\le \exp\!\left(C_A\sum_{\ell\le K}e^{-p_\ell}\right)
          e^{C_A V_K}/Z_{\rm stop}. 
 \end{split} \tag{N.22}
\]
The first estimate is uniform in volume. To see why the number of distinct returning episodes costs no combinatorial factor, work at a real pre-Mayer bracket (1.72) with the live variables fixed, as in T1 Theorem 7.146. For a returned body \(X\) let \(h_X\) count its assigned cuts. Every such cut belonged to a predecessor inside this \(X\), so \(h_X>0\) implies \(x\in X\). The simultaneous healing bodies are disjoint. The product of \(1+u(e^{A h_X}-1)\) over this bracket therefore has degree at most one in \(u\) on each compatible record. Its conditional expectation normalized by the original bracket has constant term one.

Every counted cut consumes an occupied site of the actual genealogy. The exponential tilt \(e^{A h_X}\) is paid by the geometric factor for \(\sum_j |Z_j|_j\) in (1.79), with its coefficient increased by a fixed amount as in T1 (7.214) and Lemma 7.153. The other unmarked activity and incidence reserves persist on the corresponding weak interval. The original connected sum with the extra per-return price \(1+|u|\) is nonzero for \(|u|\le c_A e^{p_\ell}\), by the proof of (7.210). A linear polynomial \(1+bu\) nonzero on that disc has \(|b|\le c_A^{-1}e^{-p_\ell}\). At \(u=1\) this bounds the conditional exponential reward for *all* cuts assigned to this release. A completely healed body no longer participates in a later assignment. The later rewards are measurable from the surviving state and later choices in T1's positive disintegration, hence these conditional bounds iterate over releases. The surviving terminal reward is inserted once in the terminal unprocessed operations and is paid by the same occupied-volume reserve. The terminal upper bound divided by its original normalization is the second factor in (N.22). Release and terminal normalization, T1 §§7.91–7.96

The signed-window trajectory gives \(\sum_\ell e^{-p_\ell}\le C\), and the fixed-endpoint Haar cylinder in T1 Proposition 7.152 gives \(Z_{\rm stop}\ge e^{-C V_K}\) on these same admissible tori. For fixed \(0<\delta<1\) the probability that the total count at a specified home exceeds \(\delta K\) is at most \(C_A e^{C_A'V_K-A\delta K}\). For the returned count alone the volume term cancels. The estimate includes many short, disjoint episodes and a single long episode on the stated records. On \(V_K=o(K)\) it beats the bare two-mark cost \(L^{8K}\) with an exponentially small remainder whenever \(A\delta>8\log L\) with fixed slack. A union over all \(V_K L^{4K}\) microscopic homes works when \(A\delta>12\log L\). The weak endpoint is chosen for that fixed \(A\) before sending \(K\) to infinity.

There is also a deterministic neighborhood version. At scale \(j\) score every distinct occupied component meeting \(B(x,Cs_j)\) once, with \(s_j\) the native physical collar size. Count all of them so that the score assigned to a given body can be computed from its own stored history. For a later release at \(\ell\), the native size comparison gives \(s_j\le C' s_\ell\). Every scoring released body contains an earlier incidence point in \(B(x,C' s_\ell)\). Disjointness and native cell packing bound their number by \(C(1+R_\ell)^4\). The release polynomial has at most this degree, and factoring it on the same zero-free disc gives an exponential price \(C_A(1+R_\ell)^4 e^{-p_\ell}\) in its logarithm. Earlier distinct components at any one cut contain distinct occupied cells, so the same stored volume pays the time charge through mergers. The new release prices are still summable on §15's trajectories. The terminal payment is the one in (N.22). Fixed home, collar and slow-volume estimates, §15

These probability assertions concern recorded physical occupancy in the positive T1 law. To use the resulting rare-event estimate for the *same* history selector in (N.1), the joint coupling with the original microscopic insertion and the cut data must be specified. With only the common original Wilson marginal an exact weaker construction is always available. Set \(q(U_0)=P_{\rm T1}(A_x\mid U_0)\) and propagate \(qF_0\) through the original kernels. Its microscopic \(L^1\) norm is at most \(B P_{\rm T1}(A_x)\), so (N.4) bounds the summed defects and terminal cross covariance for this microscopic reweighting. The complementary mark here is \((1-q)F_0\). Identifying its descendants with a path on which a particular original replacement succeeded requires a joint history argument. Histories with little occupied time may still carry an old source through many regular integrations. The full S2 estimate for that conditional mean remains open.

There is an alternative deduction for the microscopic Wilson covariance that keeps the T1 history itself. Take any nested reveal of that positive history starting with its terminal record and ending with the full record and \(U_0\). For \(U={\bf1}_{A_x}F_0^x\), \(V=F_0^y\), their conditional expectations along this reveal are martingales. Their covariance at the initial reveal plus the sum of the expectations of their conditional increment products equals \(\operatorname{Cov}(U,V)\). The proof of (N.4) applies directly to these martingales, so the sum of the *absolute conditional bracket estimates* \(E|E[\Delta U_i\Delta V_i\mid\mathcal H_{i-1}]|\) and the absolute initial covariance is at most \(C B_x B_y P(A_x)(1+\log(1/P(A_x)))\), with the continuous value at zero. This places the actual rare T1 selector on an exact decomposition of the original microscopic correlation. This reveal has its own complementary terms, and its brackets may differ from those of H7.2.

### 17. The first mixed transition and the old exterior mean

On the prefix of §5, both positive lifts use the physical sequence \([U_0],[M U_0],\ldots,[M^J U_0]\) modulo gauge. A later T1 selector may therefore be joined to this entire physical prefix by disintegration over the common microscopic Wilson field. At an arbitrary later cut, an instantaneous native geometry label can also be drawn from its true posterior given the physical cut field, provided the two unmarked cut marginals match. This gives the correct one-cut label probability. Neither coupling by itself identifies previously stored labels or the marked two-endpoint marginal across a nontrivial reset.

There is a direct comparison at a proposed matched cut. Let \(V_j\) be its physical field, \(E_j\) the complete surviving state containing \(V_j\), and \(K_{E_j}\) the true conditional law of the next physical output in the T1 lift. Include the past variables needed to screen the new draw in \(E_j\). Assume the candidate physical one-step kernel uses the same unmarked pair marginal, and suppose the old physical mark agrees up to this cut, so it is \(E(F_0\mid V_j)\). For any bounded test \(g\) of the next output the difference between its pairing with the inherited T1 microscopic mark and its pairing under the physical transition is exactly
\[
 E\!\left[\operatorname{Cov}\!\left(F_0,K_{E_j}g\mid V_j\right)\right]. \tag{N.23}
\]
This is the tower property applied first with the surviving state and then with \(V_j\). A deterministic next physical output given \(V_j\) makes (N.23) zero. LFR I pp.193–194 distinguishes the fieldwise preparation through (1.75) from (1.76), which integrates a slot in \(V_j\) and reuses it for the new draw. LFR II (1.72) retains an interaction depending on histories inside the healing body. Its equality after integration leaves (N.23) to be bounded on physical outputs. There is a local exception. Join the two histories over the same \(U_0\). If a gauge-invariant word of \(V_j\) equals the same deterministic function of \(U_0\) in each history while its determining neighborhood has had no replacement, its values agree on the intersection of those two events. For any test \(|g|\le1\) of that word, a marked expectation restricted to \(A_x\) differs by at most \(2B\min\{P(A_x),P_{\rm T1}(\text{neighborhood hit})+P_{\rm H7}(\text{neighborhood hit})\}\). Any estimate of the two hit probabilities must concern those actual neighborhoods. Source checks, two-endpoint comparisons and the full-record reveal, §18

The first ordinary mixed birth after a wholly regular prefix precedes its first eligible healing. On a wholly exterior insertion measurable on the retained variables, the first healing bracket simply transmits the exterior score. When all original compact links of the insertion are inside one admitted healing body, the proper return term of §12 has an actual rooted estimate. An insertion supported on both types of variables, or an earlier projected conditional mean, requires its own normalized comparison. In particular if \(G\) denotes the history of a strictly regular prefix, its true post-prefix numerator contains \(P(G\mid V_j)E(F_0\mid G,V_j)\). Even an estimate for the second factor on its analytic chart would leave the first factor in the later exterior integration. For a root with few rough exposures the needed restart must bound this actual emitted field in current-scale units, on the subsequent chart with its exterior and normalization. Using only the bare envelope at a late cut \(j\) can lose a factor \(L^{4j}\). The residence estimates charge occupied cuts and do not supply this current-scale source estimate.

**A first birth with a current-scale incoming mark.** There is one case where the input to the mixed operation can be identified. Fix \(b\) deterministically. Select the unresummed global prefix \(G\) on which every ordinary P, Q and R set has been empty through its input. CMP119 (3.5)–(3.9), (3.17)–(3.25) remove the redundant inherited restrictions on the new all-small region and retain its fluctuation cutoff. No healed-body coefficients have been generated on this prefix of the native joint law. Induction before any sum over alternative histories gives the bare-to-current prefix subdensity as a scalar times \(\chi_b\exp(-x_b\mathcal W_b+B_b)\) in the convention of A8.27. Dividing its marked version by this same subdensity gives the composition of the normalized *pure* regular mark maps. The probability \(P(G\mid V_b)\) remains in the outer law. This distinction matters because the resummed regular carrier can include histories with earlier returns.

Now restrict the actual next output \(W=MV_b\) to RB9's strict class, also imposing the A7 output margin that places the complete real pre-classification fiber in the common protected chart. The all-small incoming cutoff and the axial coordinates in RB9.3–RB9.5 bound its physical fluctuation by \(C g_b p_b\). Evaluation of the engineered rooted norm on that chart therefore bounds the nonconstant incoming scalar by \(h_b^{-4}M_{b,\phi}\), where \(M_{b,\phi}\) is the pure regular profile norm at that translation phase. Apply this only to the bounded, normalized N9 seed with its matching finite-volume entry, or retain the actual incoming norm explicitly.
