For \(m\) prescribed separated test cubes, the probability of their Q or eligible R failures, conditional on the selected prefix and strict \(W\), is at most \(e^{-\rho}\), \(\rho=c p_b^2 m\), by the pre-test integration and fixed-denominator comparison of RB9.20 with the pure regular potential. This calculation neither conditions on Q success before testing R nor selects a P-bad output.

For a fixed \(b\) one may subtract the pure prefix's flat value for each root and phase from that root's *entire* microscopic mark before making this split. Its full original conditional defects and covariance are then unchanged. On this convention the selected next-output numerator has \(L^1\) bound \(h_b^{-4}M_{b,\phi}e^{-\rho}\) and supremum bound \(h_b^{-4}M_{b,\phi}\). Pair it with a second prefix-regular mark on the same strict output, whose supremum is bounded at the same scale. Propagate these functions by the physical kernels only after this cut. Cauchy–Schwarz over the common translation phase and the two individual N9 second moments give, via (N.4), a total post-cut covariance cost at most
\[
 C h_b^{-8}(1+\rho)e^{-\rho} \tag{N.24}
\]
times the two bounded entry norms. For example this pays \(C r^{-8} e^{-u}\) if \(\rho-\log(1+\rho)\ge8\log^+(r/h_b)+u\). This is a bound on propagating the two specified cut functions. To count it as a subpair of the full composed H7 field requires the corresponding marked cut marginals to agree, as in (N.23). Up to the globally deterministic physical prefix the common microscopic-to-physical endpoint permits that joining. At a later unmatched cut the cut function can still be subtracted algebraically from the full H7 mark, leaving a signed residual that has no established interpretation as the complementary path event. The selected labels and the phases need not be independent. For the within-carrier innovation itself the two-copy formula \(\frac12 E[(f(V)-f(V'))(g(V)-g(V'))\mid G,W]\) cancels both flat values exactly, also when the loss is restricted to a witness in either draw. If one combines classes with different first-birth times while keeping the original subtraction, the scalar times its posterior membership weight must be retained. Equation (N.24) does not pay the cross term against an unrestricted microscopic complement and does not give a later full restart. Pure regular recurrence, N9 phase estimate and RB9 strict fiber · First-birth subdensity and normalization, §16

### 18. Which translation phases can mix a tensor into a neutral field?

Use the original independent translation digits of the *covariant cubic regular reference* in N8.8–N8.12, its covariant clover source and critical projection, and the fixed microscopic matrix of T9.52. The endpoint at one translated point is random under this average. Its deterministic mean on a stress or Weyl input and a scalar or pseudoscalar output is zero by the full signed-permutation symmetry. The following stronger statement applies to its conditional mean when only some Cartesian coordinates of the translation are known.

In a centered block, keep all or part of the digit words on \(d\) named axes and integrate the other three, two, one or zero axis words as appropriate. Signed permutations of the \(4-d\) free axes preserve this conditioning. The covariance used here is \(U_R L_{j,r} U_R^{-1}=L_{j,Rr}\) for the family \(L_{j,r}=\widehat Q_j T_r\). A single digit operator need not commute with \(R\). The complete regular kernel and the forward and backward mean graphs intertwine the symmetry. Consequently, for each sector the conditional row from the input representation to the neutral output belongs to the space of intertwiners for the subgroup on the free axes.

| Tensor input | Fewest fixed axes allowing a scalar row | Fewest fixed axes allowing a pseudoscalar row |
|---|---:|---:|
| Diagonal stress | 1 | 4 |
| Off-diagonal stress | 2 | 3 |
| Diagonal Weyl | 2 | 4 |
| Complementary-pair Weyl | 4 | 2 |
| Shared-index Weyl | 3 | 3 |

The entries are symmetry thresholds. A fixed number of axes at or above the threshold permits a nonzero row but does not establish its presence in the Wilson transport. For example, with one axis fixed, the transverse permutations have only two orbits of diagonal plaquette pairs. A diagonal Weyl tensor has zero row sums, so both orbit sums vanish. For the shared-index Weyl sector with two axes fixed, reflections eliminate a scalar row unless its two odd indices are the fixed axes. Exchanging the remaining two axes then interchanges its two shared-index entries; their difference is the Weyl component and changes sign. For the pseudoscalar projection the output changes sign under any orientation reversal, which gives the other character in the table.

One exact way to reproduce every entry is to run over the \(2^{4-d}(4-d)!\) signed permutations \(R\) fixing the \(d\) named axes pointwise. With \(P_\lambda\) from T9.14–T9.17 and \(\rho(R)J=(\Lambda^2R)J(\Lambda^2R)^T\), take the subgroup average of \(\operatorname{Tr}(P_\lambda\rho(R))\) for the scalar and of \(\det(R)\operatorname{Tr}(P_\lambda\rho(R))\) for the pseudoscalar. These are the dimensions of the two spaces of intertwiners. The table gives the first positive dimension in each column. Both counts follow by exact rational arithmetic with the seven projector matrices \(P_\lambda\). Source matrices · Conditional symmetry and the actual graph factors, §17

If the threshold is \(m\), the signed block average of this row \(Y\) against a smooth weight gains \(m\) powers of the physical block diameter \(D\):
\[
 \left\|E[Y\,\phi(x+\delta)]\right\|
 \le C D^m E\|Y\|
      \max_{i_1<\cdots<i_m}\|\partial_{i_1}\cdots\partial_{i_m}\phi\|_\infty .
 \tag{N.25}
\]
Here \(\delta_i\) depends on the retained digit word on axis \(i\) and the phase average is the original unconditioned one over the complete block. To prove it, write the test as a sum of anchored differences by inserting in each coordinate the sum of evaluation at the block center and its complementary difference. Any term using fewer than \(m\) coordinates pairs to zero by the conditional result. For a term using at least \(m\) coordinates, apply the fundamental theorem of calculus in any \(m\) of those coordinates. Each remaining difference costs at most a factor of two. This estimate concerns signed smearing. It cannot bound the squared norm of \(Y\) at an individual phase.

**A test for the unresolved point variance.** There is an exact finite-depth calculation for the other question. Expand the product of the independent \(L_{i,r_i}\) by writing each as \(\bar L_i+(L_{i,r_i}-\bar L_i)\). For a subset \(S\) of scales use the centered factor at exactly those scales, preserving the original order, and apply the same microscopic seed, normalization and terminal Hilbert-space projection to every term. For different subsets one digit appears centered in only one of the two terms, so their expected inner product vanishes. The terminal second moment equals the sum of the squared norms in expectation over *all* subsets. Dropping subsets gives rigorous lower tests. A single fixed early scale with a nonzero limiting first-order centered Weyl-to-neutral response would force an endpoint norm of at least order \(x_0^{7/11}\): its innovation is of order \(x_0^{-1}\), whereas the inverse mean normalization grows as \(x_0^{18/11}\). The tested coefficient includes the backward stable term in N8.19. The available source calculations evaluate its translation average and do not establish the required nonzero individual coefficient or a bound for the sum. The fully renormalized stress and Weyl point estimates remain open.

For a local comparison that *can* be made without the tensor variance estimate, consider the uncut flat physical Gaussian at spacing \(\eta=L^{-K}\) in terminal units. The covariance of two fine curls splits exactly into its conditional covariance and \((dM)G_c(dM)^*\), where \(M\) is the classical extension and \(G_c\) the covariance of the retained physical connection. The flat constant modes can be removed for these curl tests. A row of \(dM\) has \(\ell^1\) norm \(C\eta^2\) by the scale-normalized derivative estimates in CMP102 (19)–(21), (190). Shifting its fine root by \(n\) spacings changes that row by at most \(C\eta^2(\eta|n|)^\alpha\) for \(\eta|n|\le1\), by the Hölder estimate for the first covariant derivative. The individual coarse covariance entries are uniformly bounded by the integrable four-dimensional soft-momentum estimate of N9.12b. Thus the change in a local diagonal entry of the retained covariance correction is \(O(\eta^4(\eta|n|)^\alpha)\). This controls a flat covariance contraction. The local tensor response at nonzero external curvature also contains the movement of the constrained saddle, the measure and cutoff scores, and two external curvature derivatives. A uniform source cancels a local divergence after summation. Its cancellation at a single fine root is unproved. Gaussian comparison and the signed smooth-source estimate, §19

One part of the actual Schur response also has a fine-root gain. Fix the terminal torus and a smaller protected complex chart of coarse fields \(W\) about identity. Let \(G_K=D_W^2 S_K\), where \(S_K\) is the constrained minimum action in the fixed coarse coordinates. Tilt the fine action by a local clover tensor in physical units \(q_x=\eta^{-4}H_x\). Differentiating the constrained value at fixed \(W\) gives \(q_x(U_K(W))\), since the variation of the minimizer is tangent to the constraint. Consequently the difference of two translated sources in the coarse term \(\tfrac12\log\det(\sigma I+G_K)\) contributes exactly
\[
 \tfrac12\operatorname{Tr}\left[(\sigma I+G_K)^{-1}
 D_W^2\!\left((q_{x+n}-q_x)\circ U_K\right)\right]. \tag{N.26}
\]
The normalized fine curvature is bounded and Hölder on this chart by the regular minimizing-field estimates. In a local gauge, parallel transporting between the two nearby sites changes the normalized curvature by \(O((\eta |n|)^\alpha)\), and the clover traces eliminate the common conjugation. The product of two bounded curvatures has the same modulus. Cauchy differentiation on a smaller fixed coarse chart gives this bound for the Hessian of the *composition* in (N.26) and for two further fixed coarse directional derivatives. With \(\sigma\) chosen in O0 §13, the trace costs a terminal-volume-dependent constant. These are legitimate coarse field jets on the fixed torus. This gives an actual \(O_R((\eta |n|)^\alpha)\) difference in the coarse Schur term. The separate fine log determinant, the change of coarea and the derivative of the Lagrange multiplier multiplying \(D^2 Q\) still enter the local-source calculation. In particular the estimate does not establish that an early phase has a nonzero dressed coefficient, or that all such coefficients vanish.

## Annex O. Source transport and the scalar covariance

### 1. What the two routes establish

Both routes start with the compact Wilson measure and seek the existence-only hierarchy of §1. The observable route follows conditional expectations of insertions and their covariance defects under positive integrations. The source route differentiates sourced densities through maps frozen at zero source, retains the masses of erased components, and seeks estimates after the prescribed local vacuum subtraction.

| Question | What can be used | What still has to be shown |
|---|---|---|
| Which source survives an old integration? | The source's §4.45 transports a once-owned raw plaquette whose complete original support stays in one admitted erased component. The observable route estimates selected births and old marks in normalized histories. | A mark already integrated on an earlier step is a function or density of the retained variables. Its first and second source derivatives, their mixed products with raw marks, and their projection must be controlled on the actual mixed domains. |
| What survives after summing Gaussian shells? | source §§4.43 and 4.46 control the pinned cyclic quadratic family and the full flat quadratic source, respectively. The latter retains cross-shell terms through Schur elimination and supplies a local Gaussian vacuum. | The nonlinear constrained integrations, surviving compulsory masses and interacting terminal law require their own vacuum and normalization bounds. |
| What is known for scalar and tensor marks? | The observable route obtains all-order translation-phase moment bounds for regular scalar and pseudoscalar marks in its stated regime, and a one-smooth-insertion normalization in all 21 channels there. The source gives the full bare tensor algebra, regular estimates, and a divergent quadratic scalar contact. | All-history tensor transport, field-valued contacts, bounds uniform in insertion order, and estimates on the same phase and hierarchy are outstanding. |
| Where do the two-point routes meet? | A full normalized pointwise bound of order \(r^{-8}\) up to logarithms implies the CP.25 ultraviolet annular bound by counting \(O((r/a)^4)\) sites and inserting the lattice volume \(a^4\). | Identify the field, source placement, translation averaging, scale interval and normalization on both sides. The observable route's summed covariance defects and complementary terminal variance remain open. CP.28 also requires contact matching and CP.29b requires macroscopic smeared control. |

The source route retains the trajectory obligation B0. The source's bare coefficient \(X_{B,0}=N\beta=2N^2x_0\) agrees with §1.2. Its numerical N2-F assumption does not identify the running physical projection. The source N0 paper specifies a cofinal tiling for supplied finite trajectories. The N2 and O0 analyses exhibit a fixed-prescription obstruction to a common per-step Gaussian decrement for all ranks and give an endpoint result only for a matched fixed-depth family. Annex N §§13–15 supply a later fixed-prescription cumulative-window and weak-endpoint deduction under the cited regular estimates. Applications of the source that require stronger one-step or source-derivative inputs still require proof. In particular Annex N §11's slow-volume diagonal and Annex M's sufficient operator criterion cannot simply be used on different sequences in one existence argument. N0, Appendix C2 · N2, §6 · O0, §§12–14

Annex N §§12–14 further identify the physical terminal variance with a trace of Fisher information, give a stopped trajectory criterion conditional on signed drift windows, and evaluate one scalar determinant contribution. The heat-bath operator in §7 below uses a different conditioning from that physical terminal marginal. A bound on the constant source in §8 tests one quadratic form and does not control the trace over translated sources in Annex N §12.

**Comparison of the two routes.** For the full existence target the observable route is the more direct one. It tracks the normalized microscopic mark through the positive histories and supplies a native weak stopping prescription on its stated inputs. The source route has the sharper prescribed-coupling results for the scalar short-distance sector and for insertions during an erasure. At fixed physical separation the bare scalar covariance now has an all-history directional upper bound on the selected sequence. The required dimension-eight decay, particularly along a lattice axis, is still open. Even agreement of the scalar Fourier multiplier to every algebraic order in the bare inverse coupling, together with coordinate reflection positivity and the exact susceptibility, permits slower physical decay in the comparison field of §61. The two routes meet at the conditional mean of the actual Wilson scalar cube given two time slices. On the source route a concrete boundary-loop observable recovers the response to the entire exterior of a microscopic box with squared error smaller than its leading variance. At a fixed physical radius the same observable has a squared norm smaller than the dimension-eight scale. The physical conditional remainder is the next test for combining these results. The source calculation controls the effect of separated insertions within a fixed successful geometry; summing their contributions over different geometries with the original weights is a further step. The full tensor hierarchy also requires contact matching, energy and phase estimates. [Boundary-score identity](#25-an-exact-boundary-score-test-for-both-routes) · [Directional covariance](#45-pointwise-decay-in-each-coordinate-of-the-physical-displacement) · [Fixed-geometry source integration](#50-replacing-retained-rough-links-and-integrating-the-other-continuous-histories) · [Remaining box-boundary response](#51-changing-just-the-wilson-weights-that-couple-to-the-box-boundaries)

### 2. The source conversion on the bare lattice

Use the same four oriented and based plaquettes in each clover. For the Hermitian \(C_A\) of Annex E the source's anti-Hermitian field is \(\widehat F_A=iC_A\), so \(a^4q_{AB}=-Q_{AB}\). Write \(H\) for a smooth symmetric matrix on the six independent pairs, \(H_0=H-I\,\mathrm{tr}_6(H)/6\), and use the full matrix contraction over \(A,B\). Equations (2.10)–(2.11) give the pointwise identity
\[
 a^4 H:\widetilde q=-H_0:Q+\tfrac13\,\mathrm{tr}_6(H)\,a^4 w_a . \tag{O.1}
\]
Thus the bare clover source on this slice is \(J=-H_0\) (the ordered-index convention is \(J_{AB}=4j^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\)). The scalar coefficient on each plaquette is the average of \(\mathrm{tr}_6(H)/3\) over its four vertices. For \(H=If/2\) the clover term vanishes and the source is exactly the vertex-averaged Wilson mark with weight \(f\). The literal lower-vertex source mark differs by \(\sum_p(f(x_p)-\bar f_p)s_p\). The identity matches the sources exactly with the symmetric placement option in ICR-N1-2. Controlling the lower-vertex difference under renormalization, or extending back to both independent bare scalar directions in the 22-dimensional source, requires additional estimates. Source conventions and ICR-N1-2

### 3. One conditional contribution to the quadratic contact

**Proposition (P, bare normalized Wilson measure).** Fix \(SU(N)\), let \(d_A=N^2-1\), and take an even periodic cubic sequence with \(a\to0,\ T\to\infty,\ \beta\to\infty\). Assume the uniform local moment and tail bounds (CA.18)–(CA.20) for this sequence. The source route proves these along its compatible numerical sequence. Alternatively Annex A (3.4) gives the needed estimates directly when \(\sup\log(1+\beta)/n<\infty\), where \(n=T/a\), by covering a six-plaquette star with a bounded number of cubes and using \(W_b\le S_b\). The argument uses the real unsourced Wilson law. No interacting RG hypothesis enters beyond these bare estimates.

Fix one coordinate direction \(\mu\). Hide the positive \(\mu\)-links for which the sum of the three transverse integer coordinates is even, and let \(\mathcal E\) be the sigma-field of the remaining links. Let \(F_f=\sum_p f_p s_p\), with lower-vertex or four-vertex weights of a fixed real compact \(C^1\) test \(f\). For two such tests,
\[
 \lim_{a\to0}a^4\beta^2\,\mathbb E\operatorname{Cov}(F_f,F_g\mid\mathcal E)
 =\frac{d_A}{4}\int_{\mathbb R^4} f(x)g(x)\,dx. \tag{O.2}
\]
Consequently \(\liminf a^4\beta^2\operatorname{Var}F_f\ge(d_A/4)\int f^2\) for every such \(f\). Formula (O.2) identifies a local contribution for this particular disintegration. In the total covariance one must add \(\operatorname{Cov}(\mathbb E[F_f\mid\mathcal E],\mathbb E[F_g\mid\mathcal E])\), which can contribute even at leading order. There is no signed lower bound for arbitrary mixed tests.

**Proof.** The two parallel \(\mu\)-links of a \(\mu\nu\) plaquette have opposite transverse parities, including across the seam. Other plaquettes use no hidden link. Conditional on \(\mathcal E\), the hidden links are therefore independent under their actual six-plaquette Wilson star laws. Each incident plaquette belongs to exactly one hidden star. Write \(S_b\) for its incident energy, \(W_b\) for the minimum over that link, and \(U_*\) for a minimizer.

On \(W_b\le\delta_N\), (CP.9)–(CP.10) give a unique minimum and uniform bounds \(c\,\mathrm{dist}(Q,I)^2\le D_b(Q)\le C\,\mathrm{dist}(Q,I)^2\), where \(D_b(Q)=S_b(QU_*)-W_b\). The third derivatives in an identity chart are uniformly bounded by the explicit trace polynomial (CP.9). The Hessian \(H_b\) has spectrum in a fixed positive interval. In exponential coordinates \(Q=\exp(y/\sqrt{\beta})\), the quantity \(\beta D_b(Q)\) equals \(y^\top H_b y/2+O(\beta^{-1/2}|y|^3)\) on bounded \(y\)-sets. The chart Haar density tends to its value at zero there. The global lower bound gives a uniform Gaussian tail in the chart and an exponentially small contribution outside, also after inserting either of the first two powers of \(\beta D_b\). The denominator in these rescaled coordinates is bounded below uniformly by integrating a unit ball. These estimates give convergence of both conditional moments, uniformly over this good set, to the moments of \(y^\top H_b y/2\) for \(y\) Gaussian with covariance \(H_b^{-1}\). This variable has the law of half a chi-square with \(d_A\) degrees of freedom and variance \(d_A/2\).

The other exteriors contribute at most \((12N\beta)^2\mathbb P(W_b>\delta_N)=o(1)\) to the averaged scaled variance, by the star tail. Thus \(\beta^2\mathbb E\operatorname{Var}(S_b\mid\mathcal E)=d_A/2+o(1)\) uniformly in the root. The weighted hidden-star mark differs from \(f(x_b)S_b\) by at most \(Ca\|\nabla f\|_\infty S_b\) at the starting site \(x_b\). Conditional Cauchy–Schwarz followed by the uniform bound for \(\mathbb E(\beta S_b)^2\) gives an \(O_{f,g}(a)\) error in the averaged scaled covariance per affected star. Conditional independence adds these covariances. Only \(O_{f,g}(a^{-4})\) stars contribute. The checkerboard Riemann sum has density \(1/2\), giving (O.2). The variance of the exterior conditional mean is nonnegative, proving the diagonal conclusion. Star energy and exterior bounds · Conditional Wilson star and the original contact criterion

For a bounded renormalized second derivative of this **bare linear source**, \(\operatorname{Var}F_f-D^2C_a(0)[f,f]=O_f(1)\). On trajectories with \(a^4\beta^2\to0\), (O.2) gives the same diagonal lower limit for \(a^4\beta^2D^2 C_a(0)[f,f]\). Deterministic centering does not affect it. A running multiplicative scalar factor rescales the covariance quadratically. For tests with separated supports the conditional contribution in (O.2) is already exactly zero at sufficiently fine cutoff. The exterior term then carries their entire covariance.

### 4. Conditional contact coefficients at higher insertion orders

The same integration determines a term in every fixed-order bare cumulant. Keep the hypotheses of §3 and its checkerboard \(\mathcal E\). For \(m\ge2\) and fixed real compact \(C^1\) tests \(f_1,\ldots,f_m\),
\[
 a^4\beta^m\,\mathbb E\!\left[\kappa(F_{f_1},\ldots,F_{f_m}\mid\mathcal E)\right]
 \ \longrightarrow\ \frac{d_A}{4}(m-1)!\int_{\mathbb R^4}\prod_{i=1}^m f_i(x)\,dx. \tag{O.3}
\]
Here the conditional cumulant is evaluated first, under the normalized product of hidden-link laws. Higher derivatives of the logarithm of the **outer** integral also generate cumulants of the conditional means and of the lower conditional derivatives.

For the proof, conditional independence and multilinearity reduce the left-hand side to the sum of same-star cumulants. Exterior constants drop out at orders \(m\ge2\). On a good exterior the argument of §3 works for each fixed moment through order \(m\), since any fixed Gaussian polynomial is integrable. The energy difference converges in these moments to a Gamma variable of shape \(d_A/2\) and unit scale. Its \(m\)-th cumulant is \(d_A(m-1)!/2\), by differentiation of \(-(d_A/2)\log(1-z)\). On bad exteriors the partition formula bounds the conditional cumulant in absolute value by \(C_m(12N\beta)^m\); the true marginal tail makes its contribution vanish.

There is also a common order bound before taking the limit. For a single star let \(|X_i|\le M_i\beta S_b\). For each partition of the slots, conditional Hölder followed by conditional moment monotonicity bounds the product of absolute block moments by \((\prod_iM_i)\mathbb E[(\beta S_b)^m\mid\mathcal E]\). The sum of the absolute partition coefficients is at most \(C^m m!\), since its exponential generating function is \(\sum_{k\ge1}(e^z-1)^k/k\), finite at some positive \(z\). Averaging and using (CA.18), or the exponential bound in Annex A, gives \(C^m(m!)^2\prod_iM_i\) per star. Replace each test successively by its value at \(x_b\). Every replacement error has one factor \(O(a\|\nabla f_i\|_\infty)\), and the other factors are bounded by test suprema. Thus the total error after the \(a^4\) scaling tends to zero on compact supports, and the checkerboard Riemann sum gives (O.3). The same bound, multiplied by \(a^4\) times the number of stars touching every test support, controls the scaled left side for finite cutoff. Convergence in (O.3) is at fixed order. An outer-law estimate and a prescribed counterterm matching still enter the full R3 hierarchy.

### 5. A larger lower bound from interiors of fixed boxes

**Proposition (P, the sequence and bare real scalar source of §3).** For every fixed compact real \(C^1\) test,
\[
 \liminf_{a\to0} a^4\beta^2\operatorname{Var}(F_f)
       \ \ge\ \frac{3d_A}{2}\int_{\mathbb R^4} f(x)^2\,dx . \tag{O.4}
\]
The factor three counts free link variables per fine site in the large-box limit. This is a one-sided bound on the complete scalar variance. The proof needs local energy tails and a finite-dimensional Laplace argument at each *fixed* box size.

**Geometry and conditional law.** Fix an integer \(l\ge2\). Tile by four-dimensional boxes of side \(l\) fine spacings, keeping all links in their boundary subcomplex visible. Where the side length is indivisible, place the cuts of the periodic coordinate chart far from the fixed test supports, use complete boxes there, and reveal the leftover strip. Equivalently, one can use \(l=L^j\) for fixed \(j\) on the standard compatible sequence. A hidden edge in direction \(i\) has its three transverse coordinates strictly interior; it has \(l\) possible longitudinal starting positions. All its incident plaquettes are in the same box. Every contributing plaquette meets hidden edges of at most one box. Hence conditioning on all visible links gives an exact product of box laws. Each box has \(4l(l-1)^3\) hidden links. Gauge transformations at its \((l-1)^4\) interior vertices leave its boundary fixed. Choose a forest connecting every interior vertex to a boundary root, containing one edge per interior vertex and no path joining two roots. For fixed values of the forest links the unique interior transformation setting these links to identity acts on every other link by left and right group translations. Haar invariance and successive integration over the forest give product normalized Haar measure on the remaining
\[
 h_l=4l(l-1)^3-(l-1)^4=(3l+1)(l-1)^3
\]
free links for gauge-invariant integrands. The discarded forest group volumes are one.

**Minimum with fixed boundary.** Denote the sum of the plaquette actions meeting hidden edges in a box by \(A\). At identity boundary data its minimum is zero. A minimizer has identity curvature on every plaquette, since the other plaquettes lie on the fixed flat boundary. A flat connection on the box is pure gauge by path independence on its square faces. The boundary graph is connected. Fixing the boundary links and the interior forest to identity therefore forces every link to identity at this minimum. Its Hessian on the free links is positive definite. Indeed the quadratic form is a positive multiple of the sum of squared plaquette curls. Extending a null vector by zero on the boundary and forest gives a closed Lie-algebra-valued one-cochain on the full cubical box. It is a vertex gradient by path integration. The primitive is constant on the boundary, hence constant on the forest and all vertices. The null vector is zero.

At fixed \(l\), compactness gives a positive energy gap outside any chosen neighborhood of this unique minimum. Smooth dependence on the boundary and the positive Hessian give a unique nondegenerate nearby minimum for every boundary configuration sufficiently close to identity, using the implicit function theorem, strict convexity in a sufficiently small coordinate ball, and that gap. Apply a boundary vertex gauge first to cover an entire fixed neighborhood of the orbit of the identity boundary; changing boundary gauge preserves the conditional energy law by a change of integrated variables. The chart, positive lower Hessian bound, upper derivative bounds, and energy gap outside the chart may all depend on \(l\).

**Actual exterior probability.** In the compact space of all configurations on this one box, the sum of *all* its plaquette energies vanishes only for flat pure gauges. On the closed set where the boundary lies outside the preceding good neighborhood, this total sum is therefore bounded below by a number \(\delta_l>0\) (an empty bad set needs no estimate). Some plaquette then has energy at least \(\delta_l\) divided by the number of faces. The uniform one-star tail and a union bound give bad-boundary probability \(C_l e^{-c_l\beta}\) under the true Wilson marginal. This uses the full cube energy to estimate a boundary event; it does not assert a uniform law for every exterior.

**Evaluation and order of limits.** For good boundary data the centered, rescaled energy \(\beta(A-\min A)\) converges in each fixed moment, uniformly at fixed \(l\), to a Gamma variable of shape \(d_A h_l/2\). The proof is the rescaled-coordinate Gaussian argument of §3 on the \(d_A h_l\)-dimensional product chart. In particular \(\beta^2\operatorname{Var}(A\mid\mathcal E_l)\) tends uniformly there to \(d_A h_l/2\), where \(\mathcal E_l\) denotes all visible links. On the exceptional exteriors the corresponding expectation is \(O_l(\beta^2e^{-c_l\beta})\) by boundedness of \(A\) at fixed \(l\).

Replace all weights in each fluctuating box by \(f\) at its base vertex. The absolute error per box is bounded by \(C_l a\|\nabla f\|_\infty A\). The one-star second moment and the finite number of faces bound \(\mathbb E(\beta A)^2\le C_l\), so the averaged scaled conditional covariance error per contributing box is \(O_{l,f,g}(a)\). Visible-only plaquettes are constants for this conditional covariance. Summing independent boxes gives, by the box Riemann sum,
\[
 \lim_{a\to0}a^4\beta^2\,\mathbb E\operatorname{Cov}(F_f,F_g\mid\mathcal E_l)
 =\frac{d_A h_l}{2l^4}\int fg . \tag{O.5}
\]
Set \(g=f\) and apply total variance at each cutoff. The same full-law liminf bounds the right-hand side for **every fixed** \(l\), and \(h_l/l^4\to3\). This proves (O.4) without interchanging a volume-dependent Laplace expansion and a cutoff limit. For fixed \(l,m\ge2\), the partition-formula argument of §4 gives the analogue of (O.3) with coefficient \(d_A h_l(m-1)!/(2l^4)\). Positivity of the residual exterior variance was used only at order two. No bound on the large-box exterior covariance follows from this calculation.

In particular, when \(a^4\beta^2\to0\), the lower limit in (O.4) applies to the quadratic derivative of any prescribed counterterm yielding a bounded renormalized two-jet for the bare linear source. A matching upper estimate or a coefficient for the physically normalized field requires control of the remaining exterior.

### 6. A compact ring carrying an inherited source interaction

There is an interacting extension of the source's cyclic Gaussian calculation on a restricted Wilson geometry. Integrate \(n\ge4\) parallel links on one transverse cycle. Each integrated link has two neighbors on the cycle and four pinned neighbors. The exterior transverse transports agree across the longitudinal slab, and the other parallel links are identity. Write \(\tau=N\beta\) and \(z_p=Nj_p\). The resulting normalized-trace Wilson integral \(Z_g(w)\), \(w_p=1-z_p/\tau\), retains all its pin and edge terms. For real exterior transports the cycle holonomy is \(g\). With \(D_g(w)=\log(Z_g(w)/Z_I(w))\), the following bound holds at sufficiently large \(\tau\) on a fixed source polydisk about \(w=1\):
\[
 |D_g(w)|\le C q^n (1-\mathrm{Re\,tr}\,g),\qquad 0<q<1 . \tag{O.6}
\]
A derivative of order \(m\) of \(D_g(1-z/\tau)-D_g(1)\) in independent bounded source directions adds at most \(m!(C'/\tau)^m\) times the product of their sup norms. Constants are independent of the cycle length and coupling on this weak interval. \(Z_g(1)\) remains as the source-zero mass.

The proof uses the exact compact transfer kernel. After thermal rescaling its homogeneous operator converges in Hilbert–Schmidt norm to a Gaussian integral operator with a simple top singular mode and uniform relative gap. A small complex source perturbation preserves a cone around that mode. An exterior-square trace norm bounds the product of its dominant eigenvalue with the sum of the moduli of all the others. Together these estimates bound the inhomogeneous flat trace away from zero relative to its stated exponential-in-length normalization. Conjugation acts trivially on class functions. The twisted-minus-flat trace therefore uses only the uniformly contracting complement. Holomorphy and vanishing of the first invariant derivative at identity supply the factor \(1-\mathrm{Re\,tr}\,g\) for real \(g\). This calculation bounds a field-dependent source interaction after actual compact integrations. The full old/current reconstruction, general boundaries and the kernels in MR.27 still require estimates on their own domains. Compact Wilson ring, proof and ownership · Complementary marked kernels, MR.26–MR.28

The same method allows each matched transport its own complex neighborhood of fixed width. On class functions every transported kernel equals the flat one; on their orthogonal complement each kernel still contracts after a sufficiently small independent perturbation. This avoids a length-dependent bound on the product of complex transports. There is a local extension to mismatched exterior faces as well. If the two face transports on edge \(i\) are \(e^{D_i/2}T_i\) and \(e^{-D_i/2}T_i\), and its pin staples are \(e^{D_{i,p}}\), put \(\eta_i=\sqrt\tau\max(\|D_i\|,\max_p\|D_{i,p}\|)\). For \(\sup_i\eta_i<c\) the logarithmic change from the matched-face ring is bounded by \(C\sum_i\eta_i^2\), with the same order-dependent source factors as in (O.6). Inversion of all integrated links makes the pressure even under simultaneous mismatch reversal. Contraction of normalized transfer vectors localizes the Taylor estimate along the cycle. Thus local mismatches have a quadratic cost that can accumulate with length. Applying this estimate to general Wilson histories requires control of their actual exterior data and source readouts. Thermal mismatches, (12)–(15)

For arbitrary real face mismatches on this ring one can also bound the *value* of its pressure when all the real weights are in the same fixed interval about one. The character expansion makes the Wilson factor a positive-definite kernel of the two face assignments. Integrating the internal vertex transformations is an orthogonal projection, so the off-diagonal integral is bounded by the geometric mean of the two matched integrals. This bounds the excess log pressure over the flat comparison by \(Cq^n\). Interpolating from the matched midpoint and applying Jensen gives the lower bound with cost \(C\tau\sum_i d_i^2\), where \(d_i\) is the largest norm of the mismatch logarithms at site \(i\), in addition to the matched holonomy cost. Complex source jets at such unrestricted real exteriors remain open. All-real Wilson ring pressure, (16)

### 7. A finite-range contribution and the remaining scalar covariance

Let \(P\) average conditional-expectation updates under the full Wilson law, and let \(X_x\) be the centered dimensionless sum of the six lower-vertex plaquette energies at \(x\). With a polynomial \(p\) satisfying \(0\le p\le1\) on the spectrum \([0,1]\) and \(p(1)=1\), the bare covariance splits exactly into two positive semidefinite forms with insertions of \(1-p(P)\) and \(p(P)\) between \(X_x,X_y\). Their individual matrix entries can have either sign. The first form has finite spatial range because \(1-p(t)\) is divisible by \(1-t\), the conditional covariance for one update is local, and a polynomial of degree \(m\) propagates dependence through at most \(m\) update neighborhoods.

For the parallel single-link update, a squared Chebyshev polynomial of degree \(2k\), \(k=\lfloor(\ell_0/a)^{1/7}\rfloor\), permits replacement of the first form by a deterministic local Taylor contact \(Q_B\) through total order four with a fixed-test error \(O(\beta^{-2})\). The degree-four subtraction leaves a fifth-order Taylor remainder; the bound uses a signed operator norm on neighboring blocks before summing them. Let \(Y_f=F_f-\mathbb EF_f\), and let \(\Pi\) project onto the spectrum of \(P\) above \(1-\delta\), with \(\delta=(3\log(\ell_0/a)/k)^2\). Then
\[
 \operatorname{Cov}(F_f,F_g)
 =Q_B(f,g)+\langle Y_f,p(P)\Pi Y_g\rangle+O_{f,g}(\beta^{-2}). \tag{O.7}
\]
For separated supports the first form vanishes exactly once the cutoff is fine enough. The error in replacing their covariance by the displayed spectral term is \(O_{f,g}(\beta^{-2}(a/\ell_0)^4)\). On the even-torus eight-class update, the contact extracted by this method accounts for at least the checkerboard coefficient in (O.2) on the diagonal. Wilson heat-bath reduction, (1)–(15)

The eigenvalue parameter belongs to an auxiliary resampling kernel. Its spectral window gives an explicit remaining estimate for the interacting scalar two-jet: bound the displayed slow term after subtracting \(D^2C_a(0)[f,g]-Q_B(f,g)\) with the prescribed physical normalization. \(Q_B\) is local in the source jets, but its coefficients are computed under the torus law and require a copying or matching proof. A slow-sector contact can also remain. Neither the finite-range split nor the lower bound (O.4) gives the required upper estimate on the retained term.

There is a single choice of update and filter for which \(Q_B\) already captures the **box** lower bound (O.4). Put \(\epsilon=a/\ell_0=L^{-K}\), hide all box interiors for side \(l=L^{\lfloor K/18\rfloor}\), average the grid offset, and choose \(k=\lfloor\epsilon^{-1/14}\rfloor\). At sufficiently large \(K\), these boxes tile the compatible torus. The first form has range at most \((2k+3)(l+2)a\). The box Dirichlet form costs \(Cl^4\beta^{-2}\) in the site \(\ell^2\) estimate by a Cauchy–Schwarz sum over its plaquettes. The fifth-order Taylor remainder therefore costs \(C_{f,g}\beta^{-2}\epsilon(k+1)^7(l+2)^9=O_{f,g}(\beta^{-2})\). The same slow-window formula (O.7) holds with the new \(k\) and operator. For each fixed subdividing box side \(L^j\), the larger boxes leave fewer links visible. Their expected conditional variance dominates the fixed-box contribution in (O.5). The filter satisfies \(1-p(t)\ge(1-o(1))(1-t)\), and the Taylor error vanishes upon multiplication by \(a^4\beta^2\). Taking the cutoff liminf before \(j\to\infty\) gives
\[
 \liminf_{a\to0}a^4\beta^2 Q_B(f,f)\ \ge\ \tfrac32 d_A\int f^2 . \tag{O.8}
\]
This locates the lower bound inside an explicit local Taylor contact. The slow covariance and the matching of the computed contact to the prescribed one remain the two contributions in the residual (11). Growing-box update, (16)–(20)

For a prescribed scalar normalization that grows with the cutoff, there is room in the exponents. Choosing \(l=L^{\lfloor K/36\rfloor}\) and \(k=\lfloor\epsilon^{-1/28}\rfloor\) makes the Taylor error \(O_{f,g}(\beta^{-2}\epsilon^{1/2})\) since \(7/28+9/36=1/2\). The fast spectral error is still \(O_{f,g}(\beta^{-2}\epsilon^4)\), and the nesting argument for (O.8) still holds. A multiplicative scalar normalization \(Z_a\) multiplies these errors by \(|Z_a|^2\). In particular every fixed polylogarithmic growth preserves their vanishing on the stated trajectory. This does not identify the physical \(Z_a\) or supply the slow-sector bound for that choice.

### 8. Full constant-source susceptibility over a coupling interval

A limited upper estimate is available for the **full** Wilson law when the source is spatially constant and the bare coupling is averaged. Write \(V=n^4\), \(S=\sum_p s_p\), \(A_0=3d_A/2\), and \(\psi_n(\beta)=V^{-1}\log Z_n(\beta)\). On periodic tori with \(n\to\infty\), choose \(1<b_n<B_n\) with \(b_n\to\infty\), \(D_n=\log(B_n/b_n)\to\infty\), and \(\log(1+B_n)/(n^4D_n)\to0\). Then
\[
 \frac1{D_n}\int_{b_n}^{B_n}
 \left|\frac{\beta^2}{V}\operatorname{Var}_{n,\beta}(S)-A_0\right|
 \frac{d\beta}{\beta}\ \longrightarrow\ 0 . \tag{O.9}
\]
All couplings in an integral use the same lattice size \(n\). The conclusion gives logarithmic-measure control over a broad weak-coupling interval. It gives no pointwise upper estimate at the bare coupling selected by a prescribed trajectory.

**Proof.** The forest upper bound (CA.3), (CA.9a) and the tree-gauge lower bound (CA.6) give \(\psi_n(\beta)=-A_0\log\beta+O(1+\log(1+\beta)/V)\), uniformly in the window and down to \(b_n/2\) for large \(n\). Finite-volume differentiation gives \(\psi'_n=-\mathbb E S/V\) and \(\psi_n''=\operatorname{Var}(S)/V\). By convexity, \(0\le-\beta\psi_n'(\beta)\le2(\psi_n(\beta/2)-\psi_n(\beta))\le C(1+\log(1+B_n)/V)\) there. Thus
\[
 \int_{b_n}^{B_n}\beta^2\psi_n''(\beta)\,\frac{d\beta}{\beta}
   =[\beta\psi_n'(\beta)-\psi_n(\beta)]_{b_n}^{B_n}
   = A_0D_n+O(1+\log(1+B_n)/V). \tag{O.10}
\]
To pass to absolute deviations one also needs a uniform lower estimate. For \(f=1\), tile with \(\lfloor n/l\rfloor^4\) full boxes for each fixed \(l\) and reveal the leftover links. The conditional variances of the hidden interiors add. On the good boundary event the fixed-box Laplace proof gives \(\beta^2\operatorname{Var}(A\mid\mathcal E_l)=d_A h_l/2+o_l(1)\) uniformly as \(b_n\to\infty\). The elementary translation-invariant bound (CP.6) gives expected box energy at most \(C_l\log(2+\beta)/\beta\) at every \(n\). The positive energy cost \(\delta_l\) of a bad boundary then implies its probability tends to zero uniformly on the window. Discard its nonnegative conditional variance. This argument needs neither a fixed-site exponential tail nor a parity assumption on \(n\). Hence \(\liminf_n\inf_{[b_n,B_n]}\beta^2\psi_n''(\beta)\ge d_Ah_l/(2l^4)\). Taking \(l\to\infty\) gives \(A_0\). The negative part of \(\beta^2\psi_n''-A_0\) therefore tends uniformly to zero; (O.10) controls its signed mean. This proves (O.9). Wilson partition bounds, (CA.3), (CA.6), (CA.9a)

For each fixed tolerance, the fraction of logarithmic coupling measure on which the scaled susceptibility misses \(A_0\) by more than that tolerance tends to zero. A constant-source second derivative measures the sum over all spatial displacements. The separated-source and prescribed-contact estimates of §7 require control of how that sum is distributed in space at the selected coupling.

### 9. A conditional ring in which complex source zeros approach the origin

There is an actual compact \(SU(2)\) exterior showing why the real pressure comparison in §6 cannot in general be continued to a uniform complex polydisk of independent plaquette sources. Use the ring geometry of §6, take the shared-edge transports to be identity, and at every vertex prescribe two pin staples \(I\) and two \(-I\). This is realizable on the periodic Wilson lattice. For example, keep the exterior longitudinal links at identity and set only the two transverse edges in the third direction incident to each ring vertex on the upper face to \(-I\). Keep their lower-face partners, the fourth-direction edges, and the direction-two cycle edges at identity. These altered transverse edges are distinct for a ring on a torus with sides at least four. Inversion preserves the central element \(-I\).

With \(a(Q)=1-\mathrm{Re\,tr}\,Q\) in normalized trace, \(a(Q)+a(-Q)=2\) identically. Thus the four pins per site contribute a constant, and the conditional law is exactly the ferromagnetic compact chain with action \(\tau\sum_i a(Q_iQ_{i+1}^{-1})\). Each \(Q_i\) is Haar by simultaneous group translation. Apply the same source \(z\) to one positive pin per site and write \(M_{n,\tau}(z)=\mathbb E\exp(z\sum_i a(Q_i))\). For fixed \(n\), the edge-action minimum set is the diagonal \(Q_1=\cdots=Q_n\). Outside any neighborhood of this set the continuous nonnegative action has a positive gap. A small neighborhood where the action is less than half that gap has positive Haar volume, giving exponential concentration on the diagonal as \(\tau\to\infty\). Haar invariance determines the limiting diagonal marginal. Since \(0\le a\le2\), convergence of \(M_{n,\tau}(s/n)\) is uniform for \(s\) in compact subsets of \(\mathbb C\).

Using \(SU(2)\cong S^3\), the eigenangle of a Haar element has density \(2\sin^2\theta/\pi\) for \(0\le\theta\le\pi\). At \(s=it\) the limiting transform is
\[
 \frac{2e^{it}}{\pi}\int_0^\pi e^{-it\cos\theta}\sin^2\theta\,d\theta
   =e^{it}\frac{2J_1(t)}{t}. \tag{O.11}
\]
The positive zeros of \(J_1\) are simple. Fix a small circle about \(s=i j_{1,1}\) containing no other limiting zero. For each \(n\), locally uniform convergence and Rouché's theorem give a zero in that circle for all sufficiently large \(\tau\) in the \(s\)-variable. Taking a sequence \(\tau_n\to\infty\) and, if desired, shrinking the circles diagonally yields actual zeros \(z_n=(ij_{1,1}+o(1))/n\). Thus a logarithm uniform in length, weak coupling and every fixed real exterior fails for these independently marked pins. The construction uses one conditional exterior and a source direction resolving individual pins. It gives no zero of the unconditioned partition function for a fixed smooth scalar source on the physical trajectory. [Bessel integral](https://dlmf.nist.gov/10.9) · [Zeros of \(J_1\)](https://dlmf.nist.gov/10.21.i)

### 10. What the mixed-history and separated-source tests still require

**R1.** A ring marginal and the marked density in MR.27 condition on different data. For an unsourced joint law of internal variables \(Q\) and history output \(h\) at fixed exterior \(v\), tilt by \(e^{\lambda V_f(Q,v)}\) while holding the channel producing \(h\) fixed. Differentiating the induced *normalized* history marginal at zero gives the score \(\mathbb E(V_f\mid v,h)-\mathbb E(V_f\mid v)\). The ring integral bounds the second expectation in its specified geometry; it leaves this score unestimated. At the physical boundary the constrained coordinate change already involves \(H_{1,\Lambda_1}\) in B119 (1.22), and (1.26) integrates regular coordinates with those constraints in place. For a single real original plaquette the elementary inequality \(0\le s_p\le2N\) still bounds its unprojected marked submeasure by \(2N\) times its true mass. For MR.27 one needs the physical first and mixed second density jets after the prescribed identity and dimension-four extraction, their complex domains, and their localization across old operations. Actual remaining kernels, §6

**R2.** There is one further consequence of (O.9) on compatible sequences of tori on which the nested tilings of §7 exist and the filter has \(\sup_{[0,1-\delta]}p=o(1)\). At the constant source \(Y=S-\mathbb ES\) the Taylor subtraction is exact. Equations (O.5) and (19) of the heat-bath note, applied as a lower bound with bad boundaries discarded as in §8, give a uniform lower limit \(A_0\) for \(\beta^2\langle Y,(1-p(P))Y\rangle/V\) over the coupling windows. Positivity and (O.9) therefore give logarithmic mean tending to zero for \(\beta^2\langle Y,p(P)Y\rangle/V\). For a translation-invariant bilinear kernel \(\mathcal B\), the signed average of \(\mathcal B(f,\tau_h g)\) over *all* relative translations equals \((\sum f)(\sum g)\mathcal B(1,1)/V^2\). This identity does not give an absolute bound for any specified pair of separated supports.

Even pointwise control of the complete constant-mode coefficient would leave a quantitative obstruction. On a fixed physical torus a covariance matrix on dimensionless sites can have a contact part \(\beta^{-2}(A_0-n^{-2})I\) and a constant part \(\beta^{-2}n^{-2}\mathbf1\mathbf1^\top/V\). These forms are positive semidefinite, translation invariant and reflection positive on the scalar site algebra (they are realized at second order by independent site noise and a shared random constant). Their scaled total susceptibility per site is exactly \(A_0\) and their contact has the lower limit (O.8). For two fixed separated bumps of nonzero integrals the constant part gives order \(n^2/\beta^2\). This example tests the inference from covariance estimates; it is not asserted to arise from the Wilson link law. Bounding the actual slow overlap at the selected coupling and matching the contact in (O.7) are still necessary along this route. The final existence statement also requires the common trajectory, full tensor and all-order hierarchy, continuum covariance, energy bounds and phasewise ultraviolet laws on that same hierarchy.

### 11. A necessary quadratic contact in twenty tensor directions

For the bare 21-component field \(\widetilde q\) in Annex E define \(F_H=a^4\sum_x H(x):\widetilde q(x)\), with \(H\) a fixed smooth, compactly supported real symmetric test on \(\Lambda^2\mathbb R^4\). Let \(P_{20}\) be orthogonal projection away from the Hodge star in the Hilbert–Schmidt inner product on that source space. Along periodic Wilson lattices with \(a\to0,\ T\to\infty,\ \beta\to\infty\), there is \(c_N>0\) such that
\[
 \liminf a^4\beta^2\,\operatorname{Var}_{\mu}(F_H)
     \ \ge\ c_N\int \|P_{20}H(x)\|_{\rm HS}^2\,dx . \tag{O.12}
\]
This uses the real Wilson law and the trace completion in (2.10). In the scalar direction (O.4) gives the explicit stronger coefficient on its stated sequence. Here the rank comes from the flat Hessian of the actual local marks. The argument gives no estimate in the remaining pseudoscalar direction, and makes no claim about its full variance.

**Which flat quadratic forms can be detected.** Extend a finitely supported link potential on \(\mathbb Z^4\) by zero, perturb all links in one fixed color, and write \(dA\) for its plaquette curl. At quadratic order \(s_p=\operatorname{Tr}(dA)_p^2/2\); the linear clover curvature \(\overline{dA}\) averages the four oriented curls about a vertex. For a constant test \(H\), the spatially summed quadratic form of the trace-completed field is therefore
\[
 \sum_x H_0:\operatorname{Gram}(\overline{dA})(x)
       +\frac{\operatorname{tr}_6 H}{6}\sum_p\operatorname{Tr}(dA)_p^2 ,
 \qquad H_0=H-I\,\operatorname{tr}_6(H)/6 . \tag{O.13}
\]
The Gram matrix in (O.13) pairs the six Hermitian curvature components by the fundamental trace. In midpoint Fourier coordinates let \(c_\mu=\cos(k_\mu/2)\) and \(b_\mu=c_\mu A_\mu(k)\). The raw curl symbol is \(i(2\sin(k_\mu/2) A_\nu-2\sin(k_\nu/2) A_\mu)\) at the plaquette center. Averaging the four centers to the vertex multiplies it by \(c_\mu c_\nu\), so the clover symbol is \(i(d\wedge b)_{\mu\nu}\) with \(d_\mu=\sin k_\mu\). A contraction with the Hodge star vanishes after summation because \((d\wedge b)\wedge(d\wedge\bar b)=0\). This remains true after summing color components.

Conversely, if (O.13) vanishes for every finite link potential, polarization on pairs of coordinate delta potentials gives a zero translation-invariant finite-range Hessian, hence zero symbol. Taking \(k=t u\), dividing its quadratic symbol by \(t^2\) and sending \(t\to0\) gives \(H(u\wedge v,u\wedge v)=0\) for all real \(u,v\). Here the trace correction in (O.13) restores precisely the trace part of \(H\). In the ordered pair basis \(12,13,14,23,24,34\), tests on coordinate pairs first kill the diagonals; testing simple forms on two pairs sharing an index kills every such off-diagonal. Of the three complementary-pair entries that remain, the tests \((e_1+e_3)\wedge(e_2+e_4)\) and \((e_1+e_2)\wedge(e_3+e_4)\) impose the ratios \(1:-1:1\). These are the entries of the Hodge star. The kernel is exactly its span. The same result is detected in one sufficiently large **fixed** Dirichlet box. The kernels tested by arbitrary potentials supported strictly inside increasing boxes form nested subspaces of a 21-dimensional space. Their intersection was just computed, so one finite box attains it. Gauge fixing on a forest leaves this rank unchanged because adding an interior vertex gradient preserves every linear curl.

**Passing to the normalized, nonlinear law.** Use translates of this fixed box with disjoint collars wider than the entire clover stencil, for example one interior box per regular array of side \(2l+8\) fine spacings when the box side is \(l\). Reveal all other links, including the box boundaries. A single clover product can meet at most one hidden interior. Thus each source decomposes into an exterior term and a sum of conditionally independent functions, one for each box. At flat exterior with constant \(H\), the Hessian of a box function in the free forest-gauge coordinates is the restriction of (O.13), up to the usual factor two between a quadratic form and its Hessian. Write that matrix as \(B_H\) and the positive box-action Hessian as \(G_l\).

The actual boundary and exterior links needed by the mark lie in a fixed contractible collar together with the box. With probability tending to one their *visible* configuration is close modulo vertex gauges to the restriction of identity. Indeed a sequence of full collar configurations with total plaquette energy tending to zero has only flat limit points, all pure gauge on the filled cubical neighborhood. The expected energy there tends to zero uniformly by (CP.6). For each fixed tolerance the bad visible event therefore has vanishing probability. The comparison uses the visible data after gauge transformation, and all integrated functions are gauge invariant.

On good data choose the box minimizer and centered coordinates \(y=\sqrt\beta\,\xi\) as in §5. There is an additional issue for a tensor insertion: its first derivative at that minimizer can be nonzero. In its conditional \(L^2\) space project away the span of \(1\) and the coordinates \(y_j\), extending those coordinates boundedly in \(\xi\) outside the chart. This removes the entire possibly large linear term from \(\beta F_{H,\mathrm{box}}\). On the chart its residual before projection is the quadratic Taylor term \(\tfrac12 y^\top B y\) plus an \(L^2\) error \(O_l(\beta^{-1/2}\|H\|_\infty)\) by the uniform sixth moment and bounded third derivatives. Outside the chart the positive energy gap pays for the polynomial factors in \(\beta\). Projection only decreases variance. The moments through order four of the coordinates converge uniformly to those of the centered Gaussian with covariance the inverse action Hessian, by the fixed-box argument of §3. The Gram matrix of \(1,y_j\) is uniformly invertible. Hence the squared distance of the quadratic term from that span converges to half the squared Hilbert–Schmidt norm of the Hessian conjugated by the inverse square root of the action Hessian. This follows either from Gaussian fourth moments or by diagonalizing the conjugated matrix; odd Gaussian moments make the projection onto linear functions zero.

As the tolerated visible deviation tends to zero this expression tends to \(\tfrac12\|G_l^{-1/2} B_{H(x_b)}G_l^{-1/2}\|_{\rm HS}^2\) at the box anchor \(x_b\). All Hessians vary continuously on this fixed chart. Replacing the test by its value at the anchor changes the Hessian by \(O_l(a\|\nabla H\|_\infty)\), regardless of its linear Taylor term, which was projected out exactly. The limiting finite-dimensional norm has kernel \(\mathbb R\,{*}\) by (O.13) and is bounded below by a strictly positive multiple of \(\|P_{20}H(x_b)\|^2\). Drop bad exteriors by nonnegativity. Multiply the sum of box variances by \(a^4\), take the cutoff liminf at fixed tolerance, and then let the tolerance tend to zero. The box Riemann sum has positive fixed density. Total variance yields (O.12). For a linear bare source renormalized by a deterministic quadratic subtraction with bounded remaining two-jet, the corresponding necessary diagonal contact bound follows whenever \(a^4\beta^2\to0\). Running tensor normalization and collision terms beyond this quadratic necessity still require separate estimates.

### 12. The first nonzero local contact test for the clover pseudoscalar

For the remaining direction let \(F_{f*}\) denote the same bare field smeared with \(H(x)=f(x){*}\). Under the hypotheses of §11 there is \(c'_N>0\) such that
\[
 \liminf a^4\beta^3\,\operatorname{Var}_{\mu}(F_{f*})
       \ \ge\ c'_N\int f(x)^2\,dx . \tag{O.14}
\]
The weaker power of \(\beta\) comes from a cubic fluctuation. Equation (O.14) is a one-sided necessary contact estimate. It does not identify the susceptibility of a renormalized topological density. Perturbative calculations of additive susceptibility renormalization for a twisted-plaquette definition provide context for this issue. The proof here uses a local Taylor coefficient in precisely the clover of Annex E and an inequality under the normalized compact Wilson law. [Skouroupathis and Panagopoulos, Phys. Rev. D 72, 094509](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevD.72.094509)

**A three-link witness.** Use directions \(0,1,2,3\) in positive orientation and quaternion units \(E_i=-i\sigma_i\) in a fundamental \(SU(2)\) block, with \(E_1E_2=E_3\). In an otherwise identity configuration place \(e^{t_1E_1}\) on the direction-0 link at the origin, \(e^{t_2E_2}\) on the direction-1 link there, and \(e^{t_3E_3}\) on the direction-3 link at \(-e_2\). The coefficient of \(t_1t_2t_3\) in the **spatially summed** \(a^4{*}:\widetilde q\) equals \(1/4\). Here are the orientation and counting details. The first two links lie together in exactly one \(01\)-plaquette. Its positively based loop at zero is \(e^{t_1E_1}e^{-t_2E_2}\), with quaternion-vector coefficient \(-E_3\) at mixed degree. The third link appears in the signed \(23\)-clover at zero via the \((-,+)\) plaquette traversed along the positive third-direction link, giving signed quaternion vector \(-E_3\) at first degree. Of the four possible bases of the \(01\)-plaquette only zero has the needed transverse coordinates to see that third link in its \(23\)-clover. The third link cannot share a plaquette with either of the other two, because their displacements in direction 2 differ. Each clover contributes the factor \(1/4\) on quaternion vectors, with a common sign on conversion to Hermitian Pauli components. The color trace contributes \(2\) and the full symmetric star contraction counts \(01,23\) twice. This gives \(2\cdot2/16=1/4\). Embedding the \(SU(2)\) block in \(SU(N)\) preserves this coefficient. All skew parts used here are traceless and the star contraction has zero matrix trace.

Place the witness strictly inside a fixed box. The spatially summed flat first and second jets vanish on all interior variations, the latter by the Fourier calculation in §11. Consequently the nonzero cubic jet survives the forest gauge: the lower derivatives that could enter the third derivative under a coordinate change vanish, and a gauge transformation supported at interior vertices preserves the full observable. In particular the third-degree Taylor polynomial of the constant-profile box mark has a nonzero homogeneous cubic part in its free coordinates.

Use the independent boxes and collars of §11. On each good visible exterior project \(\beta^{3/2}F_{f*,\mathrm{box}}\) off **all** polynomials of degree at most two in the centered thermal coordinates \(y\). This removes the full constant, linear and quadratic Taylor terms on the actual conditional law, including any introduced by source placement or boundary curvature. The remaining Taylor error in \(L^2\) is \(O_{l,f}(\beta^{-1/2})\), by the fourth derivative bound and the uniform eighth moment, with the same exponentially small chart tail. Under the limiting nondegenerate Gaussian the degree-at-most-two monomials are linearly independent, and a polynomial with nonzero homogeneous degree-three part has positive squared distance from their span. Moment convergence through order six makes this distance continuous under the fixed-box perturbations. At flat data and locally constant test its square is a positive constant times \(f(x_b)^2\); varying the test over one collar changes the cubic coefficient by \(O_{l,f}(a)\). The cutoff liminf, vanishing good-set tolerance and box Riemann sum now give (O.14), exactly as in §11. Together (O.12) and (O.14) test all 21 bare directions. They leave the full separated two-point distributions and the renormalized field normalizations open.

### 13. A spatial susceptibility upper bound and a common stopped sequence

Use the closed elementary cube \(C_x\) based at \(x\). For each coordinate plane \(\alpha\) give its four parallel plaquette faces weight \(1/4\), and let \(H_x\) sum these face energies over the six planes. Each physical plaquette belongs to four cubes, so \(\sum_x H_x=S\). Write \(\mathcal H_f=\sum_x f(x)H_x\), \(\chi_n(\beta)=\operatorname{Var}(S)/n^4\), and \(A_0=3d_A/2\).

**Fixed bare coupling.** For every even periodic Wilson lattice, \(\beta>0\), and real site array \(f\),
\[
 \operatorname{Var}(\mathcal H_f)\le\chi_n(\beta)\sum_x f(x)^2. \tag{O.15}
\]
Indeed the Wilson chessboard inequality for closed cubes (Annex A) applies to factors \(\exp(t f(x)H_x)\). Face reflection maps the four plaquettes of each plane to the four faces of the reflected cube; shared boundary links are integrated once. Disseminating the \(x\)-th factor across the lattice gives \(\exp(t f(x)S)\). Take logarithms of the chessboard inequality and differentiate twice at zero, where the values and first derivatives agree by translation invariance. The same proof with six independent weights per cube bounds the vector covariance by the matrix \(\Gamma_{\alpha\gamma}=n^{-4}\operatorname{Cov}(S_\alpha,S_\gamma)\) at zero momentum, where \(S_\alpha\) sums the Wilson energies of that plane.

For example let \(B\) be the expected within-box conditional covariance for one tiling in §5, now applied to the \(H_x\) source. If \(f,g\) have disjoint supports with disjoint integration collars, then \(B(f,g)=0\). Total covariance and (O.15) sandwich the remaining form between zero and \(\chi I-B\). Subtract half of that upper form on the span of \(f,g\), where it has zero off-diagonal entry. The contraction estimate there gives
\[
 |\operatorname{Cov}(\mathcal H_f,\mathcal H_g)|
 \le \tfrac12
 \sqrt{(\chi\|f\|_{\ell^2}^2-B(f,f))(\chi\|g\|_{\ell^2}^2-B(g,g))}. \tag{O.16}
\]
The norms in (O.15)–(O.16) use counting measure. For a fixed nonzero physical test they cost order \(a^{-4}\) when squared. The rate of cancellation in these two deficits matters for an unscaled separated bound. Chessboard proof and placement analysis, §8

**Selecting the same trajectory for all tests.** Choose \(n_m=2L^m\) first and use (O.9) on the bare-coupling interval \([m^{1/4},m^{1/2}]\) to select one \(\beta_m\) with \(\beta_m^2\chi_{n_m}(\beta_m)\to A_0\). This is an existence selection based only on the zero-source Wilson susceptibility, independent of all test functions. It can be combined with the **matched native weak-stop prescription** in Annex N §§13 and 15 when its stated analytic, covariance and heat-kernel inputs hold. Start that causal recursion at \(x_0=\beta_m/(2N)\) and stop it at its first prescribed weak threshold crossing. The positive cumulative drift gives \(K_m=O(\beta_m)\), and bounded steps give \(K_m\ge c\beta_m-O(1)\). After a subsequence the weak terminal couplings converge in the compact stopping interval. Set \(a_m=\ell_0L^{-K_m}\) and \(T_m=n_m a_m=2\ell_0L^{m-K_m}\). The exponent \(m-K_m\) eventually exceeds \(K_m\), leaving room for the power-of-\(L\) divisibility and local buffers of the compatible construction. This uses the same compact group, normalization and admissible \(L\) in both arguments. The selection gives a stopped weak coupling; requiring the same exact terminal value at every depth would impose a further constraint on the susceptibility selection.

For this sequence of real bare Wilson laws and every pair of fixed compact \(C^1\) tests,
\[
 \lim_m a_m^4\beta_m^2
       \operatorname{Cov}_{n_m,\beta_m}(\mathcal H_f,\mathcal H_g)
       = A_0\int_{\mathbb R^4} f(x)g(x)\,dx . \tag{O.17}
\]
For the upper bound on each variance use (O.15) and the physical Riemann sum. For the lower bound the coefficient on a plaquette is the average of \(f\) at its four incident cube origins. In a fixed integration box it differs from \(f\) at the box anchor by \(O_{l,f}(a_m)\). The one-star moments used in (O.5) hold on the chosen parameters by the closed-cube chessboard estimate and the Wilson partition ratio since \(\log(1+\beta_m)/n_m\) is bounded. Thus (O.5) and then \(l\to\infty\) give the same lower coefficient. Polarization gives (O.17). The argument holds on this one sequence for each fixed test; it requires no choice of subsequence after specifying the sources. Without invoking the trajectory construction the same proof works for any embedding of these selected bare measures satisfying the indicated small-spacing, growing-side and local moment conditions.

For separated supports (O.17) gives \(o(a_m^{-4}\beta_m^{-2})\). The continuum two-point estimate requires the remaining covariance after subtraction and field normalization in physical units. Also the literal lower-vertex source in (CP.2) differs from the cube source: the latter averages in the two directions complementary to the plaquette. The six-plane version of (O.15) gives a sufficient correction under \(a_m^2\beta_m^2\|\Gamma|_{\mathbf 1^\perp}\|\to0\); this anisotropic susceptibility bound remains open. These restrictions distinguish (O.17) from the full R2 or S2 estimate. The two routes contribute different inputs here: the observable route supplies the native stopping geometry, and the source route supplies the pressure selection and local covariance bounds.

### 14. Transfer to the four-vertex Wilson scalar in the 21-field hierarchy

The sequence in §13 can be chosen so that (O.17) also holds for the four-vertex scalar \(a^4\sum_x f(x)w_a(x)\) from Annex E. Two points require proof. The couplings must saturate the leading **energy** as well as the susceptibility, and the error from changing the plaquette weights must be small in centered \(L^2\) on the fluctuation scale \(a^2\beta\).

**Joint selection.** In the same windows used in §13, the logarithmic mean of \(u_n(\beta)=\beta\mathbb ES/V\) tends to \(A_0\): its integral in \(d\log\beta\) is \(\psi_n(b_n)-\psi_n(B_n)=A_0 D_n+O(1)\). At fixed box side \(l\) the conditional expectation of \(\beta(A-\min A)\) on a good boundary tends uniformly to \(d_A h_l/2\) by §5. The box action \(A\) includes each plaquette meeting its hidden interior. Retain this part only on good boundaries and discard the other nonnegative energies. First take the window limit, then \(l\to\infty\); this gives \(\liminf\inf_\beta u_n(\beta)\ge A_0\). Thus the normalized logarithmic mean of \(|u_n-A_0|\) tends to zero as well. Select \(\beta_m\) where the sum of this deviation and the susceptibility deviation in (O.9) tends to zero. The geometry and stop in §13 still apply.

**Directional energy on compact tests.** For fixed smooth compact plane weights \(h_\alpha\) the selected Wilson measures satisfy
\[
 \beta a^4\sum_{\alpha,p\parallel\alpha}h_\alpha(x_p)s_p
       \ \longrightarrow\ \frac{A_0}{6}\int\sum_\alpha h_\alpha(x)\,dx
       \qquad\text{in }L^2. \tag{O.18}
\]
Here \(x_p\) can be the base or the center of the plaquette. This is a law of large numbers at the energy scale \(a^4\beta\); the smaller fluctuation scale in (O.17) is treated below.

It suffices to treat one plane and a nonnegative compact Lipschitz weight \(h\). Fix \(l=L^j\), \(j\ge1\), and use the complete aligned tiling of §5. On each fluctuating plaquette express its holonomy in a common forest slice. The exact identity
\[
 \mathbb E(s_p\mid\mathcal E_l)
 =\tfrac12\mathbb E\big(\|U_p-\mathbb E(U_p\mid\mathcal E_l)\|_{\rm HS}^2
                     \mid\mathcal E_l\big)
  +\tfrac12\|\mathbb E(U_p\mid\mathcal E_l)-I\|_{\rm HS}^2 \tag{O.19}
\]
uses \(s_p=\|U_p-I\|_{\rm HS}^2/2\). Retain its first term, and discard visible-only plaquettes and boxes whose boundary lies outside a small neighborhood of the flat gauge orbit. The expected fraction of such bad boxes tends to zero by (CP.6) and compactness as in §5. The tolerance may shrink sufficiently slowly for each fixed \(l\).

On good boundaries approaching flatness, \(\beta\) times the sum of the retained terms in one plane tends uniformly to \(d_Ah_l/12\). Indeed Taylor expansion of each matrix holonomy in the centered thermal coordinates gives its linearized curl at flat data. Its constant value cancels in the conditional variance, and the \(L^2\) remainder after multiplication by \(\sqrt\beta\) tends to zero by the fixed-box moment bounds. Summing all six planes gives the Dirichlet Gaussian energy with mean \(d_Ah_l/2\). Coordinate permutations give equal orientation means: the Gaussian curl law lives on link variations modulo interior vertex gradients, and changing the forest preserves the curls. Continuity of the linearized holonomies and of the inverse slice Hessian gives the good-boundary assertion.

The \(\beta a^4\)-weighted sum of the differences between fluctuating box energies and their conditional means has variance \(O_{l,h}(a^4)\): the boxes are conditionally independent, there are \(O_{l,h}(a^{-4})\) relevant boxes, and their \(\beta\)-scaled energies have uniformly bounded second moments at fixed \(l\). Freezing the test at each box anchor costs \(O_{l,h}(a)\) in \(L^1\). Thus the nonnegative weighted energy has lower limit in probability at least \(d_Ah_l\int h/(12l^4)\); explicitly, the probability of undershooting this value by any fixed positive amount tends to zero. Let \(l\to\infty\). The exact mean of the weighted observable tends to \(A_0\int h/6\) by translation and axis-permutation invariance and the selected limit for \(u_n\). The negative part of its deviation from this constant tends to zero in \(L^1\) by the probability lower bound, and the mean then forces the positive part to vanish in \(L^1\) as well. The one-star exponential moment gives a uniform fourth moment for the original normalized local energy sum by the triangle inequality on compact support. Interpolation gives \(L^2\) convergence. Taking positive and negative parts of the six weights proves (O.18).

**Placement on the fluctuation scale.** Take \(f\) smooth with compact support and evaluate the cube test at its geometric center. This common \(O(a)\) translation of the test preserves (O.17) by (O.15) or by its original bounds and the Riemann sum. At the center \(x_p\) of an \(\alpha=(i,j)\) plaquette the four-vertex weight averages \(f\) over displacements \((\pm ae_i\pm ae_j)/2\). The four incident cube-center weights instead average over the complementary directions \(\rho,\sigma\). Their difference is
\[
 \frac{a^2}{8}(\partial_i^2+\partial_j^2-\partial_\rho^2-\partial_\sigma^2)f(x_p)
       +O_f(a^3). \tag{O.20}
\]
After multiplying the insertion by \(a^2\beta\) the centered leading difference is an (O.18) sum with fixed directional weights, and tends to zero in \(L^2\). The remainder has \(L^2\) norm \(O_f(a)\) by the local moments. Hence the two centered insertions agree in this scaling limit. Their limiting covariances agree by Cauchy–Schwarz and (O.17). In particular, on one jointly selected sequence for the bare scalar trace of the actual 21-field family,
\[
 a_m^4\beta_m^2\operatorname{Cov}\!\left(
        a_m^4\sum_x f(x)w_{a_m}(x),\,a_m^4\sum_y g(y)w_{a_m}(y)\right)
        \ \longrightarrow\ A_0\int fg . \tag{O.21}
\]
This is the leading diagonal contact amplitude. Separated supports make its right-hand side zero, leaving an error of order \(o(a^{-4}\beta^{-2})\) in the original covariance. The required physical two-point function lies in that remainder. The unsymmetrized lower-vertex prescription has a first-order placement error; (O.20) uses both reflection-symmetric averages.

### 15. The scalar contact fluctuation has a Gaussian law on the selected sequence

Write \(Z_m(f)=a_m^2\beta_m(\mathcal H_f-\mathbb E\mathcal H_f)\), using cube centers. The selection in §§13–14 can be made so that for every finite collection of real compact smooth tests the joint law and all fixed joint moments of \(Z_m\) converge to those of centered Gaussian white noise of intensity \(A_0\). The same holds with \(\mathcal H_f\) replaced by the four-vertex Wilson scalar of (O.21). The normalization \(a_m^2\beta_m\) is essential. This limiting field measures the divergent local contact. Its separated covariance is zero; the interacting Schwinger functions sought in the existence theorem are on a different normalization.

**Conditional central limit theorem.** Fix \(l=L^j\) and subtract from \(Z_m(f)\) its conditional expectation given the visible box boundaries and all other visible links. The difference is a sum of conditionally independent centered box energies. For each fixed \(l\) its conditional variance converges in \(L^1\) to \(A_l\int f^2\), where \(A_l=d_A h_l/(2l^4)\). For constant weights in a box the uniform good-boundary conditional variance was computed in (O.5). After squaring the weights, the weighted fraction of bad boxes tends to zero in \(L^1\), using conditional Cauchy–Schwarz, the fourth star moment, and their vanishing unconditional probability. Freezing the weights in the fluctuating plaquettes costs \(O_{l,f}(a_m)\) in the scaled variance by the proof of (O.5). There are \(O_{l,f}(a_m^{-4})\) contributing boxes and the fourth moment of each scaled centered summand is \(O_{l,f}(a_m^8)\) after averaging over the exterior, by conditional Jensen and the star moments. Hence the sum of the conditional fourth moments tends to zero in \(L^1\).

These estimates give convergence of the conditional characteristic function in probability to that of the indicated Gaussian. For example, from every subsequence take a further subsequence on which the conditional variance and fourth-moment sum converge almost surely (equivalently couple the exterior laws on a product space). On almost every such array the fourth-moment bound implies Lindeberg's condition, or one can multiply the one-box characteristic functions after Taylor expansion through degree two. Boundedness gives the same unconditional limit. By (O.17) and total variance the squared norm of the conditional mean tends to \((A_0-A_l)\int f^2\). The error it makes in the characteristic function at fixed frequency is bounded by its \(L^2\) norm times that frequency. Now send \(l\to\infty\). This gives the Gaussian limit for each linear combination of tests and therefore their joint law. The \(L^2\) placement result of §14 gives the same finite-dimensional laws for \(w_a\).

**Selection for convergence of moments.** Here are details which avoid inferring moment convergence from convergence in distribution. In logarithmic coupling coordinates \(t=\log\beta\), extend \(e_m(t)=|e^{2t}\chi_{n_m}(e^t)-A_0|\) by zero outside the window. Its integral is \(o(D_{n_m})\). One may select \(t_m\) in the middle half of this window so that \(e_m(t_m)\), \(|u_{n_m}(e^{t_m})-A_0|\), and the maximal interval average \(M e_m(t_m)\) all tend to zero. Indeed the set where the maximal average exceeds \(\eta\) has length at most \(C\|e_m\|_1/\eta\): cover it by intervals whose integrals exceed \(\eta\) times their lengths, choose a disjoint subfamily with triple dilations covering the set (first on compact subsets), and sum. Markov's inequality deals with \(u_n\). Choose \(\eta\downarrow0\) slowly compared with both mean errors. The stopping argument still uses this single bare value at every selected \(n_m\).

For a real tilt \(\lambda\) put \(s_x=\lambda a^2\beta f(x)\). From the closed-cube chessboard bound (21) in the heat-bath note, after subtracting the linear term the upper bound for \(\log\mathbb E e^{\lambda Z_m(f)}\) is a sum of positive Taylor integrals
\[
 \sum_x s_x^2 \int_0^1(1-v)\chi_n(\beta-vs_x)\,dv .
 \tag{O.22}
\]
For each fixed bounded set of \(\lambda\)'s, \(|s_x|/\beta=O_f(a^2)\), so the integration remains inside the window. Under the logarithmic change of variable the **unweighted** average of \(e_m\) on each segment is bounded by \((1+O_f(a^2))M e_m(t_m)\), including segments on either side of the selected point. The Taylor weight \(1-v\le1\) gives the same error bound. Replacing \(\chi_n\) by \(\beta^{-2} A_0\) inside each integral therefore costs \(o(\beta^{-2})\) uniformly in the nonzero tilts and sites. The limiting upper bound in (O.22) is \(A_0\lambda^2\int f^2/2\). In particular all fixed real exponential moments are uniformly bounded. The joint convergence in law then gives convergence of fixed polynomial moments and of the fixed real exponential transforms by uniform integrability. For example the limiting moment of even order \(2r\) of a single source is \((2r-1)!!(A_0\int f^2)^r\).

For the four-vertex scalar the absolute value of the scaled placement error before centering is bounded by \(C_f a^4\beta\,\mathcal H_h\), where \(h\ge0\) is a fixed smooth compact majorant equal to one in an enlarged source region. This follows from the \(O_f(a^2)\) bound on the symmetric weight difference, positivity of each \(s_p\), and the finite sharing of faces. Now \(a^4\beta\mathcal H_h=a^2 Z_m(h)+a^4\beta\mathbb E\mathcal H_h\), with bounded deterministic term by §14. Equation (O.22) gives uniform bounds on the exponential moments of the absolute placement error at each fixed argument. Centering costs only a bounded deterministic factor. Hölder's inequality transfers exponential integrability and hence all fixed moment limits to the vertex-symmetric scalar. This conclusion uses neither a Gaussian approximation to the full interacting terminal law nor a zero-free disk on every retained exterior.

### 16. An actual marked readout at a constrained integration boundary

At B119 (1.26), fix an admitted first-step history and a plaquette crossing the boundary of \(\Lambda_1\). First take plaquettes whose four links are ordinary free coordinates, away from the switch (1.22) and the distinguished solved bond \(b_0(c)\). Require a connected bounded seed of integrated cluster cubes touching the plaquette. Such a mark contains both an integrated fluctuation and a retained exterior variable. On this chart the positive-link formula \(W_b=\exp(i g_0 A_b)\mathcal U_b\) is exact. Keep the entire original product in the observable, splitting off its value \(c_p\) with the **integrated** coordinates set to zero. Matrix telescoping of the product and its inverse bounds the remaining mark \(\psi_p=s_p^{\rm hol}-c_p\) by \(C g_0\sum_{b\in p\cap\mathcal I}|A_b|\) on its active cutoff. The retained \(c_p\) stays in the density as a function of exterior fields.

This admits \(\sum z_p\psi_p\) in the bounded local part of the actual B116/B119 expansion on its stated weak domain. Its input norm is at most \(C_{N,L,M,\kappa}r\vartheta\) on the fixed source polydisk \(\|z\|_\infty<r\), where \(\vartheta\) is the first-step small coordinate radius. Only the integrated seed receives the small cutoff; the exterior cubes are carried as support labels. The analytic estimate keeps the original Gaussian, all exterior factors, and the spare tree-decay margin.
